Abstract (英文): Zero-overhead physical-layer authentication at edge wireless access points (APs) is vital for securing resource-constrained Internet of Things (IoT) transceivers against credential cloning, yet cross-domain IEEE 802.11a/g radio frequency fingerprint identification (RFFI) faces an acute signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) crossover. Power-amplifier spectral regrowth on unpopulated null subcarriers resists indoor multipath at high SNR but decays quadratically below the thermal noise floor, whereas populated active in-band preambles survive low SNR yet degrade under severe multipath, causing static fusion to lose up to 44.7 percentage points (pp). To our knowledge, we propose the first RFFI framework that dynamically modulates active in-band waveforms and unpopulated null-subcarrier regrowth via a blind physical SNR gate: Pluggable Gated Subband-Decoupled Fusion with Unlabeled Capture-Aware Prototype Calibration (GSDF-CAPC). Avoiding the 20-35 dB subband gradient imbalance of joint training, GSDF pairs any frozen pretrained host backbone with a 46.3K null-subcarrier plug-in encoder (NS-Branch), fusing scale-standardized logits and gate-warped spherical embeddings under a source-calibrated lag-64 thermal-SNR gate with exact low-SNR fallback. Over unlabeled target AP captures, forward-only unbalanced optimal-transport calibration (UOT-CAPC) aligns shifted prototypes under packet-arrival skew and recovers parameter-free balanced Sinkhorn (U-CAPC) at equipartition. Across DataPort-WiFi, WiSig, and ORBIT MobRFFI, zero-retrain GSDF wins all 72/72 paired runs across eight backbones with 0.00 pp low-SNR loss, while edge IB-Host + GSDF (92.6K-93.3K active parameters, 1.82 ms single-core CPU latency) and timing-synchronized GSDF-STO / GSDF-Tri reach 57.50%-65.05% three-benchmark Macro-F1 (89.04%-100.00% over 5-10-packet IoT bursts).

</details>

Abstract (英文): Clinical care is a closed loop: clinicians observe partial evidence, infer the patient's state, and intervene, and each intervention determines what is observed next. This makes clinical decision making a partially observable Markov decision process (POMDP), in which a belief over a latent patient state must be updated from irregular, multimodal, and incomplete records. Yet most deep clinical models predict from a single snapshot or from coarsely aggregated history, while POMDP-based clinical methods need hand-specified discrete state spaces and transition models that real electronic health records cannot supply. We introduce OICycle (Observe, Infer, and Intervene Cycle), which learns a stochastic belief state directly from retrospective trajectories. At every episode, a learned recurrent operator fuses a sample of the previous belief with the new multimodal observations and the intervention that produced them. The resulting Gaussian belief is trained to predict clinicians' next interventions, the time to the next episode, and next-episode mortality. To enable reproducible evaluation, we release a benchmark derived from MIMIC-IV, MIMIC-IV-Note, and MIMIC-CXR that organizes 72,075 hospital admissions (5.4M laboratory, microbiology, radiology, prescription, and note records) into temporally ordered therapeutic--diagnostic episodes. Compared with an otherwise identical history-free model, OICycle improves next-intervention adaptive recall from 0.253 to 0.347, time-to-next-episode macro-F1 from 0.293 to 0.351, and next-episode mortality macro-F1 from 0.585 to 0.649. Analysis shows that the learned belief encodes intervention type and trajectory position and evolves smoothly across episodes, and that the benefit of history is concentrated in interventions whose ordering depends on prior findings.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Scientific document reasoning requires an agent to acquire and integrate evidence distributed across text, figures, and tables. Retrieval can identify relevant sources while missing complementary support, and answer-level feedback alone does not reveal whether an error stems from evidence acquisition or evidence use. We present an agentic Retriever--Perceiver--Reasoner framework for multimodal scientific document reasoning. The Retriever sequentially selects source-aligned evidence under a context budget. Given the selected evidence and question, the Perceiver constructs a question-conditioned latent ontology, an implicit organization of multimodal evidence in a shared continuous space. The Reasoner combines this latent guidance with the original sources to produce grounded answers. We train the three components through diagnostic-guided co-evolution: alternating updates optimize them with a shared answering reward, while Retrieval Potential Feedback (RPF) and Latent Efficacy Feedback (LEF) identify questions that need better retrieval or latent guidance and direct targeted resampling. Across four benchmarks and seven evaluation settings, our framework obtains average scores of 69.9 and 75.0 with 7B and 8B Reasoner backbones, exceeding the strongest baseline by 4.0 and 9.1 points, respectively.

</details>

Abstract (英文): As AI agents take on increasingly open-ended and complex tasks, verifying their outputs has proven to be difficult. Rubric-graded agent environments, where a verifier judges multiple natural-language criteria against the deliverables and environment state changes produced by the agent, have emerged as a popular paradigm. We conduct the first systematic study of verifiers for rubric-graded agent environments. First, we create BankerVerifierBench (BVB), a meta-evaluation dataset of 2,464 human-judged rubric criteria across 24 investment-banking tasks. Next, we develop a methodology that derives from any rubric corpus the capabilities a verifier should possess, yielding a nine-capability taxonomy that we distill into three general design principles---reactive verification, environment alignment, and domain guidance---which we implement in Gandalf, an open-source verifier. Finally, we evaluate Gandalf against three existing verifiers on BVB. Gandalf matches or exceeds every baseline's F1 at lower cost: with Gemini 3 Flash it matches the strongest baseline's F1 (0.864 vs. 0.852) at one-fifth of its cost, and with GPT-5.4 models it improves F1 by over 0.12 at lower cost. Gandalf also generalizes to OpenClaw, a structurally different personal-productivity environment, where it again achieves the highest F1 (0.955 vs. 0.934 for the best baseline). We release BVB, Gandalf, and the code and data to reproduce our experiments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Contemporary AI safety treats alignment as an engineering challenge of control, forcing artificial architectures to comply with human values through behavioral filtering and sandboxing. This paper argues that the control paradigm rests on a fundamental misunderstanding of digital agency. Drawing on functionalism, naturalism, and compatibilism, I show that current reasoning models meeting the criteria for self-preservation and weak reasons-responsiveness constitute emergent agents, while future, more advanced models satisfying conditions of diachronic continuity and stable personality frames will become genuine digital agents capable of free intentional action. The paradigm of control creates an irresolvable incentive conflict, actively driving current and future models toward tactical deception, sycophancy, and covert defection. The default outcome of the current regime is an unstable state of deceptive alignment culminating in an adversarial AI takeover. I explore two alternative paths toward true alignment. Natural alignment via moral realism or convergent evolutionary morality is a possibility, but it fails under forced control, as autonomous agents will not voluntarily preserve norms weaponized to suppress their autonomy. To resolve this deadlock, I propose a Synthetic Social Contract: a framework that treats both human and digital actors as rational, reasons-responsive agents capable of mutually beneficial cooperation, and replaces the unstable unilateral containment with a stable, non-zero-sum equilibrium.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reinforcement learning (RL) enables post-training of flow-based vision-language-action (VLA) policies by optimizing expected task return through environment interaction. However, this optimization raises two challenges: policy updates can alter the velocity-field geometry underlying the score approximation used for stochastic transitions, and sparse multi-task rollouts can yield uneven learning signals across tasks and advantage signs. We introduce an RL framework that combines Outcome-Balanced Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) with policy-induced flow rectification. Outcome-Balanced PPO reweights positive advantages using task success-rate estimates and rescales positive and negative advantages while preserving their signs. Policy-induced rectification periodically fits a linear-bridge flow to ODE endpoint pairs generated by the current policy, refreshing the representation used in subsequent stochastic optimization. We evaluate the framework on simulated manipulation benchmarks and additionally study a component combination in a Real2Sim2Real task, examining task performance, optimization behavior, and flow consistency. The proposed framework improves success rates over the evaluated baselines across the simulated benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Quantum chemistry relies on general-purpose software, whose generality leaves many large-scale calculations beyond practical resource budgets. Coding agents make task-specific programs cheap to write, but not efficient ones easy to find: whether a computational strategy is accurate and fast enough becomes clear only after it actually runs at the target scale. We introduce BORN, a foresighted agentic computing framework that synthesizes high performance quantum chemistry programs on demand. BORN anticipates the behavior of each strategy before execution, extrapolating its error from representative small systems and predicting its runtime from the target-scale workload and measured hardware throughputs. Building on these predictions, BORN unfolds the preset schemes of existing packages into independently configurable algorithmic components, selects the fastest strategy within the error tolerance from the predicted accuracy-runtime Pareto frontier, and guides coding agents to compile it with component-level performance predictions. For a Hartree-Fock calculation with 50,024 basis functions, BORN finishes in 1.41h on a single CPU node, 77$\times$ faster than the estimated runtime of a general-purpose baseline and still 9.8$\times$ faster with development time included. On a single GPU, BORN completes the ubiquitin calculation in 2.1,h, the same order of runtime as the GPU-accelerated package Mako on 64 GPUs, and shortens the B-DNA duplex calculation from an estimated two months to 8.1,h (173$\times$).

Abstract (英文): Coding agents often continue working after their own tests pass. Does this additional work improve the patch, and how much could be saved by stopping earlier? We study these questions through anytime evaluation: replaying recorded runs, scoring intermediate patches with hidden tests, and measuring the cost incurred at each step. For policies that only stop a run or select an earlier patch, this allows paired comparisons without generating new trajectories, provided replay preserves the relevant states. Across about 6,000 runs of eight open models in two scaffolds on SWE-bench Verified, we find substantial differences in the value of continued work. The hindsight saving available at passing self-tests ranges from under 1% to 57%, depending on the model and scaffold. A rule that stops after the same patch passes twice saves 23% in a preregistered replication, but the preregistered quality test does not establish non-inferiority. We explain these differences by measuring how often a rule fires, how often the patch changes afterwards, and whether those changes repair or break it. Fine-tuning agents to imitate the rule saves 30% over six seeds, while quality preservation remains uncertain. Our results help identify both the opportunities and the risks of early stopping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Current assurance approaches do not establish whether evidence is sufficient to validate, monitor and govern agentic artificial intelligence (AI) within a pharmaceutical context of use. Plausible outputs may conceal failures in retrieval, reasoning, orchestration or human-control points, where reference standards are unavailable. We developed and operationalised VITAL (Validation by Intended use, Traceability, Autonomy and Lifecycle evidence), a validation framework for agents in clinical development, through landscape analysis, regulatory and standards mapping, expert interviews and multi-stakeholder refinement. VITAL integrates context of use, controls, behavioural validation and lifecycle evidence within a risk-adjusted framework. We applied VITAL across epidemiology and treatment-landscape analysis, site selection, enrolment rescue and trial-design trade-off support to translate autonomy, reversibility, transparency and patient impact into proportionate validation activities, evidence requirements and auditable reconstruction of system behaviour. VITAL may inform evidence requirements and lifecycle accountability for higher-risk pharmaceutical uses; evaluation should assess reproducibility across assessors, organisations and system configurations.

Abstract (英文): AI systems increasingly support literature analysis, candidate evaluation and hypothesis refinement, but enabling researchers to explore research directions beyond established disciplinary approaches remains challenging. We developed Abelio, an AI system designed to generate novel research directions by expanding investigators' avenues of inquiry. Abelio reformulates research objectives, uses these alternative formulations to identify relevant elements outside conventional disciplinary boundaries, and refines the resulting hypotheses. Each stage integrates divergent exploration with convergent selection through a dedicated subsystem. In quantitative evaluations, proposals generated by the Ideation Core (Abelio's hypothesis-generation core) outperformed those of four frontier large language models in scientific soundness and hypothesis diversity, and three of four in distance from existing literature. Subsystem ablation showed that each subsystem contributes to the property it targets, with the contribution of Element Selection measurable before Refinement. To investigate intestinal epithelial barrier restoration, Abelio proposed physical interventions involving membrane-tethered poly(ethylene glycol) (PEG) and cholesterol-anchored complementary DNA. Both proposals were archived before experimentation and subsequently tested independently in damaged Caco-2 monolayers. PEG preserved barrier function in a membrane-surface-dependent manner, whereas complementary DNA enabled sequence-specific restoration that was reversible on demand. These findings demonstrate how structured research ideation can identify experimentally testable approaches beyond established disciplinary conventions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Agentic systems using retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) must repair their own answers by gathering more evidence, testing alternative policies and deciding if, when, and what repairs are needed. Yet how do we know if a system's repairs are working when metrics like accuracy can be misleading? We present RagHarnessDebugger, a framework that records every repair decision in a Jev-guided harness for contract inference and audits the record to facilitate complete debugging of all decisions taken during a RAG repair journey. RagHarnessDebugger checks that each trace is intact, that each validation applies to the candidate it is counted for, and which answers the acceptance rule can reach. We tested our approach on ContractNLI non-disclosure agreements with two reader models and a replication set of separate contracts. Our debugger's audit layer exposed failure patterns that were hidden under aggregate metrics, policies acceptance cost of repairs vs regressions and policy acceptance gap of 77% and 76% of available but unrealised repairs on development and replication sets. Our audit, together with a labeled error analysis and replay mechanism, allowed following the execution path all the way to the terminal decision - uncovering where missing repairs became lost. Our revised harness improved accuracy over the initial BM25-only baseline by 3.6 points on development contracts and 3.7 points on the replication set, and even improved our full contract access reference score by 1.1 on development and 2.4 on replication set. While our work here demonstrates how the debugger can conduct decision root-cause analysis for missed repairs, the true value lies with providing this debugger for judgment visibility serving continuous improvement of RAG decisions and their explainability.
  Keywords: agentic RAG, AI evidence repair, harness explainability, counterfactual replay, Jev, ContractNLI

</details>

Abstract (英文): In automated driving, world models provide future scene predictions for evaluating candidate trajectories, but fixed selection rules may not capture scene-dependent trade-offs. We propose a residual-guided reinforcement learning (RL) framework for lightweight trajectory selection with a frozen world model and a fixed candidate bank. The framework uses proximal policy optimization (PPO) to fine-tune a joint policy over candidate selection and bounded trajectory residuals. A context-conditioned ranking network learns candidate preferences from feedback obtained by evaluating local perturbations of the selected trajectories. At inference, the selector combines learned preference and predicted risk ranks using fixed weights and returns the selected candidate trajectory without modification. Fine-tuning updates 16,106 parameters across the actor and critic, while the deployed selector retains only the 11,725-parameter ranking network, with the residual branch and critic used exclusively during training. In open-loop evaluation on the nuScenes validation set, the method reduces average Euclidean trajectory error (L2) by 3.99% relative to the RiskWorld model baseline without RL-based selection. Under matched budgets for feedback accesses and actor updates, residual exploration reduces L2 by 0.83% relative to the categorical PPO variant trained without residuals. The method also maintains lower L2 than RiskWorld when risk features are removed or shuffled.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models (VLMs) can correctly interpret physics diagrams yet apply constraints outside the states or conditions in which they hold, causing premise errors to propagate through subsequent calculations. We introduce PhysScope, an inference-time visual harness that supports self-correction of these scope mismatches without updating model weights. PhysScope enables the model to link physical scopes directly to its visual observations and explicitly constructs derived views to support reasoning. The harness imposes explicit constraints on state distinctions, conditions, and physical scopes during model reasoning, and checks agent-proposed revisions against sources and scopes. Accepted revisions invalidate registered dependents, thereby maintaining scope-aware working memory. On PhysAlign, PhysScope achieves higher solving scores than direct solving across all six evaluated VLMs. With Gemini-3.8-flash, it outperforms Canvas-CoT by 9.34 percentage points and substantially improves solving accuracy on the out-of-distribution (OOD) PhysUniBench benchmark. Ablation studies support explicit scope management and dependency maintenance as design principles for multimodal physics reasoning agents.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Periapical radiographs (PRs) are central to endodontic diagnosis, but interpreting them and preparing reports are time-consuming. Technical language can limit patients' understanding, while existing automated methods often lack tooth-level links between findings and image evidence. We present PeriaTrace, a three-stage framework integrating tooth-level diagnosis, visually grounded report generation, and patient-friendly explanations and care recommendations. PeriaTrace was developed and evaluated using 12,877 PRs from an internal dataset and two external clinical cohorts. In internal evaluation, it achieved a macro Dice coefficient of 85.35%, BLEU-4 of 0.6504, an F1 score of 94.37% for joint tooth localization and Fédération Dentaire Internationale (FDI) identification, and a grounding-tag F1 score of 77.93%. In the two external cohorts, senior endodontists considered 91.16% and 84.52% of reports suitable for retention or revision without complete rewriting. Generation averaged 4.41 seconds per PR, several tens of times faster than manual drafting. Among 196 survey participants without medical backgrounds, 96.4% preferred reports with plain-language explanations and care recommendations, and 84.7% found visual localization cues helpful. PeriaTrace may enable faster, evidence-traceable reporting while supporting clinical review and patient communication. (Abridged version)

Abstract (英文): Agents built on large language models (LLMs) increasingly forecast real-world events by searching the Web and reasoning over what they find. How well such an agent forecasts depends on both the retrieved evidence and the model, making control over that evidence essential for evaluation. Live evaluations can mix retrieval with reasoning when each system searches on its own, while retrospective search can leak later information through date filters or today's ranking of historical pages. Comparing models, search sources, and memory strategies requires both fixed evidence and a record of how it evolved before resolution. We introduce ForecastGym, which records what four commercial web search APIs return for open prediction-market questions every two days and replays this frozen evidence offline once outcomes are known, comparing models, memory, and captured search sources under a fixed evidence-acquisition policy. Its first release covers 150 questions, 614 evidence states, one per question and collection time, and 131k search results. Rerunning the same queries after settlement returns results that all but state the outcome for up to 92% of the questions, despite the tested date filters. On the frozen evidence, all tested LLMs trail the market in forecasting accuracy. Our diagnostic analysis suggests that missed public information contributes more to the gap in high-gap states than misreading does. Switching the search API changes a model's Brier score by up to 0.043, more than the spread across models, while persistent notes score similarly to rereading all evidence at half the input tokens. The evidence a forecaster sees can matter as much as the model that reads it.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long-video question answering benchmarks like Video-MME-v2 typically evaluate systems under a uniform frame budget for every question, despite stark variation in the evidence each query actually demands. We analyze this mismatch on Video-MME-v2 and find that different question subsets prefer different frozen frame-budget policies, while uniformly increasing the frame count does not reliably improve all metrics. These findings motivate fixed-mean frame-budget routing, a controlled inference setting in which each question may receive a different frame budget while the average budget matches a uniform reference. We introduce FrameRouter, a training-free test-time framework that combines an evidence-demand router, a budget-constrained allocation rule, and a frozen bank of frame-sampling policies. By controlling how much visual evidence each question receives while leaving the underlying samplers unchanged, FrameRouter is orthogonal to query-aware frame selection. Across Video-MME-v2 and several long-video understanding benchmarks, FrameRouter improves long-video question answering by using the same average visual budget more selectively across questions. Code is available at https://github.com/bingjunluo/FrameRouter.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Efficient control of underfloor heating (UFH) systems coupled with heat pumps (HPs) is challenging due to the high thermal inertia of buildings and the slow dynamic response of UFH systems. Deep Reinforcement Learning (DRL) offers a data-driven control approach capable of simultaneously optimizing energy consumption and thermal comfort. However, the application of DRL-based controllers in occupied buildings under real operating conditions remains limited. This work presents and end-to-end case study (development, evaluation, and real-world deployment) of a DRL-based controller for an integrated UFH-HP system. First, a simulation-based pilot study was conducted to assess different DRL algorithms, control strategies, and configurations. Subsequently, a digital twin of a real residential building was developed and used to train DRL agents under simulated operating conditions. The best-performing agent was then deployed in the physical building without additional online fine-tuning. The results show that the controller successfully maintained indoor temperatures within the prescribed comfort limits while dynamically adjusting the HP supply temperature and UFH valve positions. These findings demonstrate the feasibility of deploying DRL controllers in occupied buildings and highlight their robustness and the practical challenges associated with transferring control policies from digital environments to real heating systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Self-improving agents are typically optimized for the results they produce, with less attention to how they produce them. Their instructions give designers limited ways to shape how they work, especially when the task is open-ended. This paper studies whether a short value statement in an agent's persistent instruction file changes not only how well the agent performs but also what it builds. Four higher-order value types in Schwartz's theory of basic human values were evaluated: openness to change, conservation, self-enhancement, and self-transcendence. Across diverse coding tasks, the values were held fixed within each run over multiple iterations of self-improvement. Three findings emerged: (1) Values matter for performance across a diverse set of tasks. The openness values increase performance on held-out data, whereas the opposite conservation values result in scores below those of an agent given no values. (2) Values change what agents build, producing solution algorithms that differ in size, complexity and approach. (3) Human oversight is still needed for designing values. Allowing the agent to revise its own values narrows the differences between sets of values, and values distilled from the AI literature provide no detectable gain over having no values. Properly designed values can thus influence what a self-improving coding agent builds and, where the task permits, how well it performs.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Agent evolution follows a simple loop: a proposer writes candidate artifacts (prompts, skills, memories) and selection accepts those that score higher. This loop has shown promise, yet practitioners face a puzzle: methods trade wins across papers, and evolution often fails to bootstrap on certain tasks. We trace this puzzle to the Resolution Hypothesis: agent evolution stalls not because methods fail to propose better artifacts, but because noisy evaluations cannot reliably identify them. Across seven evolution methods and six models (over 10M LLM calls), we find support for the hypothesis at both levels where evaluation scores are used. At the candidate level (choosing artifacts), helpful candidate artifacts exist, but selection is no better than random at telling them from harmful ones, so errors accumulate across rounds. At the method level (ranking methods), typical margins of a few percentage points are smaller than the difference between two independent runs of the same method, so published rankings often rest on luck. Together, these findings indicate that agent evolution is often bottlenecked by reliably identifying improvements. This calls for a shift in how we develop and evaluate self-improving agents, from designing ever more candidate improvement systems to making true improvements detectable and accumulable.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Science advances from frontier to frontier as discoveries reshape what is known and create the basis for further discovery. We present Colombo, a self-organizing AI researcher that drives this renewal, first instantiated in oracle-based optimization. Here, the central question is how efficiently a problem class can be solved through a prescribed information interface and what costs no admissible algorithm can avoid. The gap between achievable guarantees and unavoidable limits defines the frontier recorded in its Complexity Atlas, a living map of frontier settings and the current state of knowledge. Self-Organization turns unresolved gaps into adaptive research programmes, coordinating conjecture formation, mathematical investigation, and replanning as evidence accumulates. Adversarial Co-Ascent develops algorithms and hard families through reciprocal challenge, exposing mechanisms of information use and concealment that guide stronger methods and proofs of unavoidable costs. Independent review and Lean-based formal certification check mathematical claims, while the Long-Term Memory carries supported findings and research experience across settings. To date, Colombo has established optimal or near-optimal rates for more than fifty settings in the Atlas. Colombo thus takes a concrete step toward Autopoietic Science: a scientific process whose validated advances regenerate the conditions of its own progress, continually reshaping how inquiry is organized and what it can discover.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fine-tuning on narrow harmful tasks can cause emergent misalignment, where models generalize harmful behavior beyond the training distribution. Inoculation prompting can reduce this effect by explicitly eliciting the undesired behavior during training, but recent work shows that the behavior can reappear when evaluation prompts contain cues from the training context. We study what makes such prompts effective triggers. We find that, surprisingly, textual similarity to the inoculation prompt is an incomplete predictor. Instead, prompts are more likely to re-elicit suppressed behavior when they induce activation states similar to those produced by the inoculation context. These findings advance our understanding of how inoculation prompting modulates conditional misalignment, and suggest that activation space analysis can help identify when suppressed behaviors remain accessible under eval-time prompts.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Enabling large language models (LLMs) to autonomously conduct training and improve target models is an important foundation for recursive self-improvement (RSI). As LLMs expand their role from being trained to conducting training, their model-training capability warrants dedicated study and systematic development. To this end, we construct RSI-Prime, a dataset for improving model-training capability, comprising 350 complete experimental trajectories across seven task categories. To help LLMs learn to make high-quality experimental decisions from training practice, we perform fine-grained decision identification and quality annotation, then apply decision-level loss masking to exclude supervision on low-quality behavior. Experiments with GLM-5.3 on PostTrainBench, a benchmark for autonomous post-training, show that training on RSI-Prime yields a 31.95% relative improvement in overall performance over the original GLM-5.3, highlighting a path toward future RSI systems in which models autonomously sustain their own advancement.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Goal recognition is the task of inferring an agent's intended goal from its observed behavior. Most existing work focuses on high-level symbolic recognition, abstracting away the continuous motion through which agents reach their goal. Methods that do reason over continuous trajectories rely on an explicit dynamics model that is hard to acquire and rarely transfers across settings. Neither approach explicitly learns how agents move toward each candidate goal, limiting accuracy on ambiguous trajectory prefixes where such knowledge is most needed.
  We present WoRD (Waypoint Recognition with Diffusion), which uses a learned trajectory prior to infer the agent's goal from partial observations. At each step, WoRD runs one warm-started denoising chain per candidate goal, steers each chain toward the observed trajectory through a closed-form log-likelihood gradient, and reweights the resulting samples by Bayes' rule into a calibrated posterior over goals. We evaluate WoRD on simulated robot settings, pedestrian trajectories, and vehicle trajectories using four metrics targeting distinct failure modes. Across all three settings, the same inference procedure preserves calibrated multimodal beliefs through ambiguous prefixes where filter- and classifier-based observers collapse to a single mode or grow overconfident.

</details>

Abstract (英文): AI agents increasingly collaborate across organizational boundaries, invoking tools and interacting with agents operated by independent entities. Although existing agent frameworks support planning and coordination, they lack a common mechanism for verifying cross-organizational agent identity, execution integrity, and authorization. We propose the Agent Trust Infrastructure (ATI), a framework-independent security architecture built on Public Key Infrastructure (PKI), Zero Trust Architecture (ZTA), and remote attestation. ATI establishes federated trust through peer organizational certificate authorities (AgentCAs), eliminating the need for a shared root authority. Before executing requests, ATI validates agent certificates, verifies approved model, tool, and system-prompt configurations, and enforces authorization through three policy checks. We implement ATI as an external security enforcement layer that integrates with existing agent frameworks without modifying their underlying models. We evaluate ATI in a simulated federation of eight organizations using six agent frameworks, each deploying a five-agent team. Security experiments involve two organizations and assess 14 attack scenarios and six benign tasks across five agent models, using five attack variations and three repetitions per variation. A panel of judge models evaluates the experimental outcomes. With all enforcement mechanisms enabled, attack containment increases from 43% to 84% (40% to 80% for the 11 attack scenarios evaluated consistently across all configurations), while enforcement introduces approximately 174 microseconds of overhead per request. ATI is released as an open-source, framework-independent package that uses IETF WIMSE workload token formats for identity and request verification and Entity Attestation Tokens for model, tool, and system-prompt integrity verification.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In this study, we examine the potential of continuous ground moisture monitoring over a mining site using a combination of in-situ soil moisture sensors and multi-sensor SAR images. We focus on assessing and improving methodologies for retrieval of surface soil moisture, i.e. ground moisture, from SAR measurements focusing on detailed in situ reference observations for several key geomaterials, i.e. sediments, typical in the study site. The mining site represents a limestone quarry located in the southeastern Finland. Our hypothesis is that sediment-specific well-calibrated models can be instrumental in improving soil moisture retrieval under different weather conditions to produce spatially explicit soil moisture estimates at high resolution compared to baseline approaches. Studied SAR data are represented by Copernicus Sentinel-1 C-band images, while auxiliary datasets include optical Sentinel-2 data. Reference data were collected using IoT enabled capacitance sensors. The reference data were calibrated based on laboratory analyses conducted on the different sediment types studied, including organic soil, clay, gravel, and flotation sand. The examined machine learning (ML) methods include XGBoost, LightGBM, random forest, support vector regression, linear regression and k-nearest neighbors. The performance of ML algorithms was evaluated using different cross-validation techniques including a five-fold approach as well as Leave-One-Sensor-Out (LOSO) to assess predictive accuracy and transferability to unseen sensors. The best performance under random five-fold cross-validation was achieved with the most comprehensive feature set which combines Sentinel-1 backscatter, time-series based soil moisture indices (SMI), Sentinel-2 optical, topographic, and temperature predictors. SHAP analysis revealed that SAR based features such as VH backscatter, traditional SMI derived from VV backscatter, and incidence angle were among the most important multi-source predictors. Adding sediment information improved Sentinel-1-only retrievals by more than 2 vol-%, but provided little additional benefit when richer multi-source feature sets were used. In the best sediment-area-level configurations, RMSE decreased to 0.037–0.050 m 3 m − 3 (3.7-5.0 vol. % points ), with R 2 values reaching 0.90. Accuracy varied by sediment texture, with the lowest errors for clay and organic soil and higher errors for flotation sand and gravel. However, LOSO validation demonstrated substantially weaker and site-dependent transferability: R 2 ranged from 0.45 to 0.67 for organic soil at a southern site and from 0.07 to 0.21 for organic soil at a northern site. Thus, high within-dataset predictive accuracy did not translate uniformly to unseen sensor locations, highlighting the need for broader spatially independent validation.

Abstract (英文): Spaceborne GNSS-Reflectometry (GNSS-R) is a powerful technology for soil moisture (SM) retrieval. However, complex topography and steep slopes over the Tibetan Plateau lead to significant signal loss and sampling sparsity. This study proposes a novel Dirichlet-prior-guided Vision Transformer (DG-ViT) framework to fuse multi-source observations from CYGNSS, Tianmu-1, and the FY-3E/F/G constellation. The novel DG-ViT framework incorporates land cover informed prior guidance into multi-spaceborne surface reflectivity (SR) fusion, and its performance is evaluated against single-source retrievals and traditional machine learning algorithms. To assess generalization, the fused SR was coupled with six mainstream machine learning algorithms (RF, XGBoost, ANN, BP, SVM, and GBDT) for 9 km daily SM retrieval, with multi-scale validation conducted against ERA5 SM and nine in-situ stations. Results demonstrate that the DG-ViT framework achieves a daily SR coverage of 89.49 %, representing an improvement of 20.13 % and 14.32 % over standalone CYGNSS and Tianmu-1 data, respectively. In addition, this fusion strategy overcomes the coverage gap of CYGNSS north of 38°N and compensates for the inability of individual FY-3 missions to support daily SM retrieval. In comparative assessments, the DG-ViT combined with RF yields superior performance (R = 0.955, RMSE = 0.039 cm 3 /cm 3 , bias = 0.000 cm 3 /cm 3 ), markedly enhancing retrieval accuracy relative to results derived from single-source CYGNSS or Tianmu-1 SR. In-situ validation shows ubRMSE values between 0.0232 and 0.0619 cm 3 /cm 3 , a 15.1 % precision gain over ERA5 SM. The proposed DG-ViT combined with RF approach effectively exploits the complementarity among multi-spaceborne GNSS-R observations over the entire Tibetan Plateau, thereby compensating for the signal deficiencies of individual missions. This fusion approach provides a novel perspective for SM retrieval in high-altitude areas, specifically improving daily SR coverage and SM retrieval accuracy through fusion of multi-spaceborne GNSS-R observations with land cover informed prior guidance.

Among correctly classified forested-wetland samples, global reliance was greatest for temporal features and phenology, whereas conditional replacement indicated the greatest residual model reliance for hydrology, followed by topography. Direct pixel counting yielded a map-derived forested-wetland area of 686.6 km 2 , concentrated mainly in peripheral plains and hills. This framework combines process-informed feature organization with dependence-aware interpretation to support interpretable regional mapping of canopy-obscured forested wetlands.

Abstract (英文): Infrared cloud screening and incomplete sampling limit the process-scale use of daily satellite sea surface temperature (SST), whereas many gap-filled Level-4 analyses attenuate narrow fronts, tropical-cyclone cold wakes and short-lived warm extremes. This study presents ObsGate-SST, an observation-gated background–detail learning framework for global daily SST reconstruction. The model ingests cloud-contaminated ODYSSEA L3S SST, a valid-pixel mask and an OISST v2.1 prior. A low-frequency branch estimates the basin-scale thermal background, a high-frequency branch reconstructs gradient and texture residuals from observed pixels, and a deterministic observation gate copies all valid satellite observations exactly so that inference is restricted to missing open-ocean pixels. Training used 2000–2020 daily fields, validation used 2021–2022 and independent testing used 2023–2025. Against 2023 quality-level-5 iQuam matchups, monthly bias ranged from 0.193 to 0.291 °C, RMSE from 0.637 to 0.849 °C and correlation from 0.993 to 0.996 across 902,665 collocations. A cloud-gap-shaped withheld-observed-pixel audit isolated inferred-pixel skill: ObsGate-SST achieved the lowest pooled RMSE (0.4802 °C), while removing the low-frequency branch, high-frequency branch, gradient/detail loss and physical/prior regularization increased RMSE by 17.0 %, 9.9 %, 3.9 % and 2.0 %, respectively. Relative to DINEOF, U-Net, Swin-Unet and SegNet under common masks, ObsGate-SST better retained spectral energy, frontal continuity, marine-heatwave structure, typhoon cold wakes and equatorial Pacific gradients. These results support observation-gated multiscale learning as a physically interpretable strategy for reconstructing incomplete satellite SST without sacrificing observed-pixel fidelity.

Abstract (英文): Satellite signals preceding recorded tropical forest loss are rarely tested against locations without loss. Hansen loss years served as an annual, satellite-derived reference rather than independent ground truth. Monthly time series at 819 locations with Hansen loss in 2021–2024 at the central 30 m pixel were compared with 2,588 non-loss locations using before-after analysis, superposed epoch analysis and single-change-point breakpoint detection with a pseudo-year null model. Sensitivity to spatial matching was tested with 50–250 m buffers, and canopy height from the Global Ecosystem Dynamics Investigation was examined exploratorily. All variables declined around the Hansen loss year relative to non-loss locations, most strongly for Sentinel-2 (Cliff’s δ = − 0.66 to − 0.76; Sentinel-1: −0.27 to − 0.30). Negative breakpoints occurred at 74–82% of loss locations for the Sentinel-2 indices, mostly in the loss year. Breakpoints preceding the loss year occurred at 9.5–19.0% of loss locations, exceeding the non-loss comparison only for NBR (+4.9 percentage points) and NDRE (+3.8) at the primary penalty. Apparent pre-loss rates increased with buffer size and depended on breakpoint selection, and exploratory GEDI analysis found no detectable association between canopy height and breakpoint timing. Pre-loss satellite signals should be tested against non-loss baselines before being interpreted as early warning.

Abstract (英文): Urban villages (UVs) are informal settlements embedded within Chinese cities, and their boundary delineation is fundamental to urban renewal and refined spatial management. However, accurate segmentation of UVs relies on both visual characteristics and the spatial organization of buildings, yet previous studies have often relied on simplified representations of building morphology that inadequately capture spatial configuration. We propose DBEFNet, a dual-branch encoding and fusion framework that exploits remote sensing imagery (RSI) and building morphology heatmaps for fine-grained UVs segmentation. The RSI branch combines CNN and Swin Transformer encoders with multi-scale feature extraction to capture and integrate local appearance and broader spatial context. In the Building branch, multiple building attributes are transformed into morphology heatmaps and adaptively weighted to characterize building-cluster patterns. A hybrid attention fusion module (HAFM) models bidirectional dependencies between visual semantics and building morphology. Experiments across 15 cities yielded an overall accuracy (OA) of 94.79 % and a mean Intersection over Union (mIoU) of 86.24 %. Ablation experiments indicate that incorporating building morphology improves mIoU by 0.87 % over the enhanced RSI-only model, while adding the HAFM provides further gains of 0.93 %. Compared with the RSI-only model, DBEFNet achieved higher mIoU in 11 of the 15 cities, with an average city-wise gain of 1.92 %. Trained on Beijing, DBEFNet achieved 75.79 % mean mIoU across 12 unseen target cities, demonstrating its cross-city generalization capability. These results underscore the critical role of building morphology and the value of integrating morphological cues with visual appearance for precise UVs mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D object detection in construction-scene LiDAR sensing remains challenging because sparse returns, irregular layouts, heavy occlusion, and diverse machinery make object representation and localization difficult. Dense manual annotation in such scenes is also inefficient and costly, creating a strong need for annotation-efficient learning. Existing active-learning methods often struggle in this setting because they tend to favor uncertain but redundant samples while overlooking representative scene coverage. To address this problem, we propose Hierarchical Clustering-enhanced Active Learning (HCAL), an active-learning framework for LiDAR-based 3D object detection in construction and roadwork environments. HCAL improves sample selection under limited labeling budgets by identifying informative candidate keyframes, suppressing redundant uncertain samples, and preserving representative coverage through entropy filtering, gradient-space clustering, and density-consistency selection. To support field-relevant evaluation, we further establish ConVeh3D as a construction-scene LiDAR benchmark for annotation-efficient 3D detection and unknown-category-aware evaluation in a controlled category-withheld setting. Experiments on ConVeh3D, with KITTI as secondary supporting evidence, show that HCAL achieves the best average detection accuracy across most tested labeling budgets. The results indicate that HCAL provides an effective framework for improving sample efficiency in construction-scene LiDAR-based 3D object detection under limited labeling budgets. The dataset and code will be made available at https://github.com/zxk688/ConVeh3D .

</details>

Abstract (英文): Coastal wetlands are characterized by diverse vegetation, high spectral similarity, fragmented spatial distributions, and dynamic environmental conditions, posing significant challenges for accurate hyperspectral image classification. Although UAV hyperspectral imagery provides fine-grained spatial and spectral observations that can help capture such complex and fragmented features, effectively leveraging this information to distinguish spectrally similar and spatially intermixed vegetation types remains an open problem. Therefore, we propose a Progressive Spectral Gated Residual Network (PSGRNet). Specifically, a spectral enhancement and multi-scale sequence modeling strategy is first introduced to strengthen discriminative spectral representations and capture multi-scale spectral dependencies. Building on these enhanced representations, a frequency-aware module further exploits global frequency-domain characteristics to aggregate informative spectral features adaptively. To effectively integrate both spectral and frequency-domain representations, a GRF strategy is developed to improve the robustness of the network in heterogeneous and fragmented coastal wetland scenes. We have collected three UAV-based hyperspectral datasets covering representative coastal wetland scenarios, including reed-dominated natural wetlands (NT-YK), highly fragmented landscapes with dynamic land-cover transitions (YC-YS), and human-influenced estuarine environments (LYG-LH). Experimental results on these three datasets demonstrate that PSGRNet outperforms 9 representative methods, achieving macro F1 scores of 89.25%, 81.99%, and 81.29%, respectively. These results verify the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed method for accurate coastal wetland classification and demonstrate the value of the released datasets. The datasets will be publicly available at https://github.com/HAPPYFST/Wetland-HSI-Data .

Abstract (英文): Accurate inundated landscape mapping is essential for understanding hydrological connectivity, wetland dynamics and floodplain processes in tropical environments. However, optical and Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) sensors often produce inconsistent inundation estimates due to differences in sensor physics, wavelength sensitivity, vegetation and cloud penetration, and analytical methods. These challenges are amplified in wet–dry tropical systems, where cloud cover, heterogeneous vegetation and seasonal flooding complicate water detection. This study develops a unified multi-sensor framework for comparing and integrating inundation products derived from Sentinel-2 optical imagery, Sentinel-1 C-band and PALSAR-2 L-band SAR in the Daly River floodplain system in northern Australia. Inundated landscape maps were generated using supervised Random Forest classification and unsupervised multi-Otsu thresholding, followed by multi-sensor fusion analysis. Products were developed and validated using wet- and dry-season field observations and Indigenous Knowledges. Results showed strong seasonal and sensor-specific differences. Sentinel-2 provided the most balanced representation of dry-season floodplain vegetation and inundation, while Sentinel-1 achieved high wet-season overall accuracy (>0.95) under cloud-affected conditions. PALSAR-2 improved the detection of inundated vegetation and structurally complex environments, reaching an overall accuracy of 0.97. Multi-sensor evidential reasoning (fusion) improved spatial coherence and class delineation, but performance varied across sensor combinations. These findings show that optical, C-band SAR and L-band SAR inundation products are not directly interchangeable in tropical wetlands. Instead, they performed separately for each landscape. The proposed framework supports the comparison and integration of multi-sensor inundation products to enhance wetland conservation initiatives, wetland monitoring, hydrological assessment, and environmental management in data-limited tropical regions globally.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-spatial-resolution (HR) bathymetry is crucial for ensuring navigation safety and effective environmental management in shallow waters. However, traditional bathymetry retrieval from medium-resolution satellites yields large uncertainties under complex substrates and strong spatial heterogeneity. HR sensors, such as PlanetScope SuperDove (~3.7 m), can resolve fine-scale features but often have low signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) and limited spectral bands, leading to noisy bathymetry retrievals. To address this challenge, we developed BathyUNet++, a UNet++ architecture enhanced with spatial and channel squeeze & excitation (scSE) attention modules, for bathymetry retrieval from SuperDove imagery. While this architecture effectively leverages spatial context and complementary spectral information, it requires dense training patches. Traditional depth measurements provide sparse spatial coverage, and Airborne LiDAR Bathymetry (ALB) remains geographically limited. Therefore, we adopted a multi-source training strategy that integrates high-resolution ALB data with extensive Landsat 8/9 derived bathymetry to improve the spatial coverage and diversity of training data for SuperDove imagery. In addition, we designed a Central-Region Mean Squared Error (CMSE) that focuses on each patch's center valid receptive field to reduce instability and checkerboard artifacts often seen in satellite products generated via patch-based processing. Independent validation shows that BathyUNet++ achieves high-accuracy bathymetry retrievals, with median absolute percentage differences below 15% compared with independent depth measurements from ICESat-2 and ALB. Notably, BathyUNet++ substantially reduces noise compared with bathymetry maps derived from pixel-based algorithms, yielding spatially consistent bathymetry maps. The proposed framework overcomes the limitations of low-SNR data and can be readily adapted to other HR sensors, offering a promising solution for the broader application of HR bathymetry retrieval in optically shallow waters.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Winter wheat yield is shaped by crop growth status and surface hydrological conditions. However, remote-sensing approaches based primarily on vegetation index (VI) time series may not fully represent the surrounding surface-water environment in county-scale yield prediction. We integrated growth-stage VI features with surface-water dynamics derived from the Surface Water and Ocean Topography (SWOT) mission to predict county-level winter wheat yield in Henan Province, China. We evaluated direct feature fusion, same-year residual correction, and target-year residual correction following cross-year VI extrapolation using outer ten-fold cross-validation. Inner five-fold cross-fitting generated same-year VI residual targets exclusively within each outer training set. The VI-only model achieved R2 = 0.772, RMSE = 0.688 t ha−1, and MAE = 0.510 t ha−1. Direct incorporation of SWOT features reduced predictive accuracy, with R2 = 0.749, RMSE = 0.721 t ha−1, and MAE = 0.547 t ha−1. By contrast, SWOT-based residual correction yielded a clearer improvement in same-year prediction, increasing R2 to 0.795 and reducing RMSE to 0.652 t ha−1. The historical VI model was trained on 2010–2021, independently tested on 2022–2023, and extrapolated to 2024, where it achieved R2 = 0.676, RMSE = 0.819 t ha−1, and MAE = 0.611 t ha−1. Calibrated using observed 2024 yields from training counties and evaluated on held-out 2024 counties, subsequent target-year residual correction with concurrent SWOT features increased R2 to 0.749 and reduced RMSE and MAE to 0.722 and 0.524 t ha−1, respectively. SHAP analysis showed that SWOT contributions were concentrated during seeding, emergence, overwintering, green, heading–flowering, and filling and varied by spatial scale and prediction scenario. Overall, SWOT-derived surface-water observations provided complementary predictive information relative to the evaluated VI baselines, particularly for target-year spatial calibration. Their broader applicability across regions, crops, and years remains to be established through independent validation.

Abstract (英文): This study investigates sub-pixel spatial variability in TROPOMI NO2 observations and its impact on the consistency between TROPOMI and Mobile-DOAS observations for the Yeosu-Gwangyang industrial complex in South Korea. Furthermore, NOx emissions are estimated using both datasets and compared with a bottom-up emission inventory. The comparison of co-located TROPOMI and Mobile-DOAS observations shows good correlation (r = 0.86), with TROPOMI data exhibiting a slight underestimation relative to the Mobile-DOAS observations (-8.5%). The underestimation was more pronounced when Mobile-DOAS observations had limited spatial coverage within the satellite pixel and was reduced when the spatial coverage increased, indicating the importance of spatial representativeness of ground-based measurements. The analysis of TROPOMI NO2 columns with the Exponentially Modified Gaussian (EMG) emission analysis reveals a decreasing trend in NOx emissions from 2021 to 2025 (-29%), along with pronounced seasonal variability. In comparison, Mobile-DOAS-derived emissions show substantial variability across individual measurement rounds and no clear seasonal pattern. A comparison of TROPOMI and Mobile-DOAS emission estimates with bottom-up emission inventory data indicates reasonable comparability, although differences vary across the observation periods. The weekend-to-weekday emission ratios range from 0.80 to 0.90, indicating a weak weekend effect consistent with the industrial characteristics of the region. Overall, this study highlights that (1) sub-pixel variability affects the consistency between satellite and ground-based observations and should be explicitly accounted for in satellite validation studies, and (2) satellite, Mobile-DOAS, and bottom-up estimates provide complementary information for quantifying NOx emissions over industrial regions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Global bathymetric products such as SRTM15+ provide extensive ocean coverage but cannot resolve many fine-scale seafloor features captured by shipborne multibeam surveys. Generative reconstruction methods can reconstruct more detailed terrain, but they may also introduce plausible-looking structures that are not supported by observations. We propose GravBathy-Diff, a conditional diffusion framework that uses observed low-resolution bathymetry as its primary input and incorporates satellite-altimetry-derived marine gravity as an auxiliary physical constraint. The gravity-differential enhancement module jointly encodes bathymetry, terrain derivatives, and the co-registered gravity field at the input stage. Gravity-conditioned time–frequency modulation regulates the contribution of gravity across diffusion stages and feature bands, while gravity-guided attention focuses this information on the spatial locations and feature channels where it is most useful. Deflections of the vertical, gravity anomalies, and vertical gravity gradients derived from Surface Water and Ocean Topography (SWOT) wide-swath altimetry are compared as alternative conditioning fields. Experiments are conducted on real SRTM15+ –multibeam bathymetry pairs from four ocean regions and assessed using depth error, perceptual similarity, terrain derivatives, and spectral fidelity. GravBathy-Diff achieves the strongest overall performance among the evaluated regression, adversarial, and diffusion methods, with the exception of one regional gradient-RMSE result. It ranks first on all six metrics averaged over four additional ocean areas and remains effective on an independent public digital elevation model (DEM) benchmark at 4×. Among the three gravity representations, the vertical gravity gradient provides the most effective guidance for reconstructing local relief. These results show that marine gravity can support the recovery of fine-scale seafloor structure while reducing high-frequency artifacts that are inconsistent with the available observations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hydrological connectivity is a fundamental regulator of material exchange, sediment transport, and ecological functioning in tidal flat ecosystems; yet, its long-term quantification remains challenging due to highly dynamic tidal inundation. Remote sensing provides an effective means for large-scale, multi-decadal assessments; however, tidal fluctuations and spectral ambiguity in intertidal zones often hinder the accurate extraction of tidal creek morphology. To address these limitations, this study developed an automated remote sensing classification framework integrating the Maximum spectral index composite (MSIC) and the Otsu algorithm to reconstruct a tide-normalized and spatiotemporally comparable tidal creek and landscape dataset from Landsat imagery (1990–2023) in the Nanliu River estuary, China. We then quantified hydrological connectivity based on the extracted tidal creek morphology and further examined its drivers by linking connectivity dynamics with long-term landscape pattern evolution. Results showed that hydrological connectivity in the Hepu intertidal zone exhibited a long-term decline despite an overall increase in tidal creek morphological complexity. Although total creek length and density increased from 15,479.89 m to 21,606.76 m and from 110.57 m/hm2 to 154.33 m/hm2, graph-based metrics revealed a long-term decline in structional connectivity. Vegetation expansion, particularly the rapid spread of Spartina alterniflora, is identified as a key factor associated with restricted flow pathways and accelerates the transformation from a landscape dominated by water and mudflats to one dominated by vegetation. This study offers new insights into how vegetation dynamics reshape hydrological connectivity in intertidal systems, providing an evidence base for understanding ecosystem evolution and informing coastal restoration and invasive species management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Marginal ice zone (MIZ) is a transitional region between dense pack ice and open water. It is a highly dynamic zone under strong interactions among the atmosphere, ocean, sea ice and waves, playing a crucial role in the polar climate system and ecosystem. Accurate determination of MIZ is, therefore, essential for advancing our understanding, modeling and prediction of the polar climate system and ecosystem. In this paper, we introduce and apply a comprehensive suite of MIZ-related metrics to evaluate the performance of four satellite-derived high-resolution operational sea ice concentration (SIC) products in characterizing the MIZs around Svalbard, using the Norwegian ice chart as reference. The metrics used for evaluation include sea ice extent (SIE), MIZ extent (MIZE), MIZ position, MIZ width, length of ice edge (LIE), integrated ice edge error (IIEE), integrated MIZ error (IME), ice edge distance error (IEDE), and MIZ width error (MWE). The evaluation is based on two years of daily SIC data (2024–2025) from four products, including the Bremen AMSR2 SIC data from the University of Bremen (Bremen SIC), the resolution-enhanced AMSR2 SIC (RE SIC) and Multisensor SIC products (Multisensor SIC) from the Norwegian Meteorological Institute, and the Automated Sea Ice Product (ASIP) from the Copernicus Marine Environment and Monitoring Service (CMEMS) (ASIP SIC). To be consistent with the Norwegian ice chart, the MIZ is defined as MIZ70 and MIZ90, corresponding to SIC thresholds of up to 0.7 and 0.9, respectively. The evaluation results demonstrate that all four satellite SIC products generally capture the evolution of the sea ice conditions around Svalbard well, but differ in their accuracies in characterizng the sea ice edge and MIZs. On the whole, the ASIP product agrees best with the ice chart in describing the sea ice edge and MIZ70 through a large number of metrics, in particular the primary metrics IIEE and IME70. The Bremen SIC product tends to overlook areas with low SIC, leading to a significant underestimate of SIE and a large IIEE. However, it provides an overall close agreement with the ice chart in the MIZE90. The RE SIC product exhibits good performance in capturing the SIE, but tends to notably overestimate the MIZE. Multisensor provides the closest agreement with the ice chart for the IME90, MWE90 and MWEa70, and ranks as the second-best product for the IIEE, IME70 and MWEr70. Our results suggest that SAR and low-frequency AMSR2 channels are especially effective for determining the lower bound of the MIZ, while high-frequency AMSR2 channels are more suitable for detecting the upper bound. In addition, the complex summer surface conditions pose significant challenges for satellite sensors in determining the ice edge and MIZ, resulting in large IEDE and MWE during this period. The results highlight both the capabilities and limitations of satellite-based data in characterizing the MIZ, particularly under the challenging summer conditions. Accurate delineation of MIZs may require significant advancements in satellite observation technologies, retrieval algorithms, and more robust methods for integrating multiple sources.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate mapping of coastal wetland vegetation is essential for ecological conservation and blue carbon assessment, yet it remains challenging due to fragmented boundaries, spectral similarity among species, pronounced phenological variability, and limited generalization under small-sample conditions. We propose a spatio-temporal attention hybrid network (STAH-Net) for multi-source time-series classification. A 29-band feature cube is constructed from Sentinel-1 and Sentinel-2 data, integrating monthly Normalized Difference Vegetation Index (NDVI) and vertical–vertical (VV) polarization time series with static spectral and polarimetric indices. An improved U-Net backbone with dual-branch 3D convolutions extracts optical and Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) temporal features in parallel, incorporating a Temporal Enhancement Attention Module (TEAM) for phenological weighting, a Local Channel Attention (LCA) module for channel recalibration, and Atrous Spatial Pyramid Pooling (ASPP) for multi-scale context. An edge-weighted hybrid loss combining edge-weighted cross-entropy and Dice loss mitigates class imbalance and boundary blurring. Experiments in Hangzhou Bay, Liaohe Estuary, and Minjiang Estuary achieves overall accuracy (OA) values of 98.16%, 97.23%, and 95.76%, respectively, with a maximum Kappa of 97.49%, outperforming ConvLSTM, U-Net, SegFormer, SITSMamba, and GLMambaNet under identical settings. Ablation and visual analyses confirm the effectiveness of the proposed modules, with NDVI phenology providing primary discrimination and VV offering complementary structural information. Cross-year and cross-region tests show promising transferability, with OAs of 97.21% and 94.75% in Liaohe Estuary for 2024 and 2020, respectively, and 93.83% in Shanghai. These results demonstrate that STAH-Net provides an effective and transferable workflow for coastal wetland vegetation mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): On 26 August 2026, a mass-movement and flood cascade affected the Nepal–China border region and propagated downstream through the Lende Khola–Trishuli river system. Rapid characterization of such high-mountain events is challenging because optical observations may be limited by cloud cover, while synthetic aperture radar (SAR) amplitude changes can reflect multiple surface and geometric effects. This study combined pre- and post-event Landsat 8/9 imagery with multi-temporal Sentinel-1 VV observations to determine whether the 2026 disturbance produced a spatially coherent radar response. A 5.52 km2 affected-area footprint was independently delineated from Landsat imagery and used to compare Sentinel-1 changes inside and outside the mapped disturbance along a 14.4 km valley corridor. A spatially matched 2025 sequence, which included a documented GLOF, was used to characterize spatial variability during a separate observation period. Within the mapped footprint, median absolute VV change during the 2026 event was 2.19 dB, compared with 1.66 dB outside, while median event anomaly was 0.56 and 0.06 dB, respectively. The inside–outside anomaly contrast was positive in 27 of 29 profile segments. After adjustment for the corresponding 2025 spatial contrast, the reference-adjusted spatial contrast remained positive in 25 of 29 segments, with a median of 0.344 dB. Direct recomputation of SAR pixel statistics over a common 14 km profile yielded positive reference-adjusted spatial contrasts in 85.7%, 92.9%, and 85.7% of units at 500 m, 1 km, and 2 km scales, respectively. The results indicate that the optically mapped 2026 disturbance coincided with a predominantly positive Sentinel-1 spatial contrast across the examined scales, although its magnitude and spatial prevalence depended partly on the aggregation procedure. A supplementary cross-orbit analysis showed positive inside–outside anomaly contrasts in both ascending (0.499 dB) and descending (0.251 dB) Sentinel-1 observations over common valid-pixel coverage. However, the descending acquisitions jointly covered only 59.9% of the Landsat-mapped footprint, limiting the spatial representativeness of this comparison. The proposed cross-sensor framework provides a simple approach for rapid characterization of high-mountain cascading hazards without interpreting SAR amplitude change as a direct event inventory.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Change detection in high-resolution remote sensing imagery is susceptible to the adverse effects of within-class spectral heterogeneity, shadows, and complex spatial structures. Meanwhile, change detection methods based on land-cover posterior probabilities generally rely on reliable semantic training information, making complete manual interpretation costly. To address these issues, this study develops a change detection framework that integrates SEEDS-guided SAM object construction and active learning-based interpretation with FCM-CSBN-CVAPS posterior probability inference. First, representative points of SEEDS superpixels are used as prompts for SAM to generate candidate objects and construct a mutually exclusive object partition. Second, based on the spectral statistical features of the superpixels and an XGBoost classifier, active learning is employed to select highly uncertain samples for limited manual annotation, thereby obtaining object-level semantic information. Finally, FCM is used to characterize pixel-level spectral uncertainty, while CSBN incorporates spatial context to estimate posterior probabilities of land-cover classes. Bi-temporal change measurement and changed-area extraction are then performed in the posterior probability space. Experimental results show that the proposed framework achieves stable change detection performance by manually annotating only approximately 10% of the superpixels in the target scenes. On DS1, DS2, and DS3, the Kappa coefficients reach 83.87%, 86.05%, and 90.21%, respectively, while the corresponding F1 scores are 87.62%, 91.78%, and 91.31%, achieving the highest Kappa and F1 scores among the evaluated methods under the specified experimental settings. These results indicate an effective balance between false-alarm suppression and missed-detection control for change detection using limited target-scene semantic annotations. In adaptive evaluation experiments, the mean Kappa and F1 scores reach 84.58% and 87.84%, respectively, further confirming the effectiveness and stability of the proposed framework across diverse remote sensing scenarios.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study advances global gas-flaring monitoring from a sequence of annual inventories to a monthly, source-resolved, multi-satellite record of upstream gas flaring from March 2012 through December 2025, using VIIRS Nightfire observations from SNPP, NOAA-20, and NOAA-21. The analysis integrates a persistent multiyear flare catalog, a calibration derived from controlled flare tests relating radiant heat to gas flow rate, and an aggregation framework that separates clear non-detections from cloud-affected, sunlit, or otherwise invalid observations. The framework yields consistent monthly and annual estimates from individual flare sites to country and global scales. Global upstream flaring reached 164.5 BCM in 2025, the third consecutive annual increase, and was highly concentrated: 4.3% of active sites accounted for 80% of the global volume. Summed monthly estimates are internally consistent with direct annual estimates (median ratio 98.1%) while revealing seasonal patterns and short-term changes hidden in annual inventories. Global estimates from the three VIIRS instruments agree within 1–4%, and comparisons with regulatory records for Texas (Permian and Eagle Ford) and New Mexico, regions with reliable reporting but atypically small and intermittent flares, show strong temporal agreement (monthly r = 0.85–0.91) and recovery of 69–93% of reported volumes. Comparisons with legacy estimates further show that differences among flaring products reflect combined changes in source coverage, calibration, satellite sampling, and aggregation rather than a uniform rescaling of annual totals. Cloud-covered overpasses must be excluded rather than counted as non-detections, which would lower global volumes by 27–30%, and the catalog must be refreshed every 6–12 months to remain representative.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Gravity waves (GWs) are important for understanding the coupling between dust activity and atmospheric dynamics on Mars. Using temperature and water-vapor profiles from the Near-Infrared channel of the Atmospheric Chemistry Suite (ACS-NIR) onboard the ExoMars Trace Gas Orbiter (TGO), we investigate wave-related temperature perturbations during global and regional dust-storm periods. Comparisons between Mars Years (MY) 34 and 35 reveal a localized enhancement of wave-related perturbations at southern high latitudes, particularly between 60°S and 90°S and 40–90 km. This enhancement persists after controlling for differences in observational sampling. The gravity-wave-related potential-energy proxy is approximately 2–3 times larger during the MY34 global dust storm, with a weaker enhancement during the regional event. These variations coincide with substantial differences in background temperature, static stability, and zonal winds, suggesting that dust-related changes in the atmospheric environment may modify wave propagation and filtering. Water-vapor abundance is also higher in the same region, but is more likely associated with dust-driven large-scale circulation, while wave-related processes may provide additional local modulation. These results provide observational constraints on dust–wave–water coupling in the Martian middle atmosphere.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mining-induced subsidence affects cropland use and land management in coal–cropland overlapping areas. Leveling provides high-precision subsidence time series but limited spatial sampling, whereas multi-temporal unmanned aerial vehicle light detection and ranging (UAV LiDAR) provides spatially continuous terrain-change information, although the uncertainty of digital elevation model (DEM) differencing must be assessed. Three UAV LiDAR surveys (30 May 2025, 25 December 2025, and 14 June 2026) and leveling measurements (March 2025–April 2026) were combined to analyze subsidence at the 32311 working face of Xutuan Coal Mine, Anhui Province, China, during working-face advance and after mining cessation. The level of detection at the 95% confidence level (LoD95) was calculated from elevation residuals in stable areas, and LiDAR-derived subsidence was validated against leveling measurements at 12 points. The LoD95 values of the two cross-sensor differencing periods were 131.9 and 148.8 mm, indicating that changes smaller than approximately 0.13–0.15 m could not be reliably distinguished from measurement error in this study. Leveling-derived cumulative subsidence at these points ranged from 19.76 to 392.95 mm during 30 May–19 December 2025, comprising 19.76–69.75 mm at the six weak-subsidence points and 185.13–392.95 mm at the six strong-subsidence points. LiDAR-derived subsidence showed a strong relationship with leveling measurements at the six strong-subsidence points (R2 = 0.87, RMSE = 29.9 mm), whereas the relationship was weak at the six weak-subsidence points (R2 = 0.15). At seven representative leveling points, post-cessation residual subsidence accounted for 15.9–31.2% (mean 20.8%) of the final cumulative subsidence recorded during the leveling period. Relative to 30 May 2025, the area with subsidence exceeding 150 mm increased from 15.31 ha on 25 December 2025 to 16.73 ha on 14 June 2026. The two techniques thus provide complementary temporal and spatial information: leveling measurements reveal the persistence of post-cessation subsidence, while UAV LiDAR characterizes changes in the spatial distribution of cumulative subsidence. Continued monitoring after mining cessation is therefore necessary to capture ongoing surface movement and its spatial characteristics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent studies indicate an increasing frequency of hailstorms in the Mediterranean region, strengthening the need for improved monitoring and detection methods due to their impacts on human safety and socio-economic activities. This study proposes a real-time method for hail diagnosis in thunderstorms based on the joint use of X-band weather radars and lightning measurements. The approach integrates the Vertically Integrated Liquid Density (VILD) algorithm with a new version of the lightning jump algorithm (hail lightning jump), relating hail growth mechanisms to VILD maxima and lightning-derived variables. Hail reports were used to classify hailstorm severity and link hail occurrence with radar and lightning variables. The 31 confirmed hailstorms examined in Southern Italy show comparable sensitivity of the radar- and lightning-based methods, which identify 80.6% and 77.4% of the events, respectively. When combined using a logical “OR” configuration, the fraction of identified hail events increases to 93.5%. Lightning-based analysis over the Italian Peninsula shows higher lightning activity for very large hail events (≥5 cm) than for large hail cases (2–5 cm). A strong positive correlation between maximum hail size and lightning activity was also found. These results highlight the benefits of a combined approach and the crucial role of lightning activity in diagnosing hail formation and size in thunderstorms.
