Abstract (英文): Forested mountain catchments play a critical role in regulating regional water resources, yet the effects of land-use change and precipitation–temperature perturbations on internal water partitioning and its seasonal and spatial variability remain insufficiently understood. In this study, we integrated remotely sensed land-cover information and MODIS-derived normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI) with a calibrated and validated Soil and Water Assessment Tool (SWAT) model to examine hydrological sensitivities and non-additive responses under three land-use configurations and controlled climate perturbations in the upper Pi River Basin, China. Land-cover transitions affected only 2.7% of the study area and produced relatively small hydrological differences among land-use configurations, whereas a 20% precipitation increase enhanced soil-profile percolation (PERC) and surface runoff (SURQ) by 28.62% and 49.81%, respectively; 2 °C warming increased actual evapotranspiration (ET) by 3.58% but reduced SURQ by 5.39%. Under the combined scenario of 20% higher precipitation and 2 °C warming, ET and SURQ increased by 7.15% and 42.06%, respectively, with a non-additive SURQ deviation of −2.36 percentage points; ET sensitivity to precipitation peaked in August, coinciding with the NDVI maximum, whereas sensitivity to warming peaked in May, and soil water storage (SW) responses to warming differed in direction among subbasins. These findings reveal contrasting influences of precipitation supply and temperature-related evaporative demand on hydrological pathways, with component-specific non-additive effects and distinct seasonal and spatial responses. These patterns support the use of remotely sensed vegetation information and distributed hydrological modeling to identify climate-sensitive periods and evaluate spatial differences in water availability in forested mountain catchments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Cross-platform land surface temperature (LST) harmonization is often evaluated by agreement of individual observations, although urban analyses commonly rely on spatial differences and temporal changes. Using Advanced Baseline Imager observations from Geostationary Operational Environmental Satellites (GOES)-16, -17, -18, and -19 over western North American cities during 2019–2026, we evaluated five cohorts using established mean-squared-error (MSE) decomposition and cross-validated model selection at each measurand. Component root-mean-squared error (RMSE) decreased by 29.3–59.7%, whereas core–ring contrast RMSE ranged from an 11.5% reduction to a 13.1% increase. Loss of residual covariance offset 94.6–114.0% of gross variance-plus-bias gains. In the post hoc 2026 temporal evaluation of the same 75 cities used for 2025 calibration, core and ring RMSE declined by 59.7% and 56.1%, but contrast RMSE reduction was −0.14% (95% crossed city–interval bootstrap interval, −6.75% to +6.48%, with cities and intervals resampled independently). The historical 2021 evaluation likewise showed only a small, uncertain contrast improvement (2.9%; 95% interval, −4.3% to +9.8%). Calibration-based selection retained raw contrasts and transitions, while a residual interval rule withheld uncertain transition direction claims. Independent three-station radiometric comparisons assessed raw satellite products at station scale rather than the complete urban workflow. These findings demonstrate why harmonization performance should be evaluated at the derived scientific quantity, with processing decisions based on its observed cross-platform consistency.

Abstract (英文): Mesoscale eddies and internal solitary waves (ISWs) influence upper-ocean chlorophyll variability, yet their roles in the spatial variations in chlorophyll during their interaction remain poorly revealed. Using satellite ocean-color and sunglint imagery, we observed different chlorophyll variabilities in three cases: an ISW-only case, an eddy-only case, and an eddy–ISW interaction case. In the ISW-only case, chlorophyll exhibited coherent, banded, and phase-dependent anomalies, characterized by reduced concentrations near the wave trough but enhanced ones in the trailing region, with a chlorophyll anomaly reaching approximately 0.02 mg m−3. In the eddy-only case, chlorophyll exhibited a distinct low-core but high-periphery structure, with elevated filamentary features concentrated around the eddy boundary and spatially consistent with enhanced finite-size Lyapunov exponent (FSLE). During the eddy–ISW interaction, the ISW crests became strongly deformed near the eddy periphery, consistent with modulation by the mesoscale eddy. Within the eddy interior, the ISW-related banded chlorophyll structure remained evident, whereas near the eddy periphery these bands became curved and spatially overlapped with filamentary chlorophyll structures. The difference in chlorophyll between the eddy–ISW and eddy-only cases increased toward the eddy periphery, reaching approximately 50% at ~1.2R. These observations reveal a multiscale spatial modulation of chlorophyll variability, in which the mesoscale eddy establishes a broader spatial pattern, while ISWs induce propagating fine-scale perturbations. The eddy–ISW interaction primarily regulates and enhances chlorophyll variability near the eddy periphery, rather than producing a uniform response throughout the eddy.

</details>

Abstract (英文): SAM2 has great potential for remote sensing water body extraction due to its strong feature representation capability. However, when directly applied to the water body segmentation task, SAM2 still faces several challenges, including domain discrepancy, spectral confusion between water bodies and shadow-like objects, significant intra-class variations, and dependence on manual prompts. To address these challenges, this paper proposes the PMA-SAM2, which integrates domain prior injection, prototype memory calibration, and automatic prompt guidance for robust water body extraction. First, a Multi-level Prior Adaptation Module is designed to inject spatial geometric information and texture features into the SAM2 encoder, enabling effective alignment between general visual representations and remote sensing domain features. Then, a Prototype Memory Calibration Module is introduced to dynamically accumulate water feature prototypes under different geographical environments and imaging conditions, improving the model’s robustness to intra-class variations and inter-class spectral ambiguity. Furthermore, an Automatic Prompt Decoder is proposed to transform prototype-retrieved similarity maps into dense prompts, replacing manual prompts and enabling fully automatic water body segmentation. Experimental results on four datasets demonstrate that the proposed method achieves superior water body extraction accuracy compared with mainstream segmentation methods, verifying its strong generalization capability and scene adaptability.

Abstract (英文): The 4 December 2025 Mw 6.0 Aheqi earthquake occurred on the Maidan–Shayilamusi Fault system in the western southern Tian Shan, approximately 23 months after the 2024 Mw 7.0 Wushi earthquake. We used ascending- and descending-track Sentinel-1A differential interferometric synthetic aperture radar (DInSAR) observations to map coseismic line-of-sight (LOS) deformation, jointly invert fault geometry and distributed slip, calculate Coulomb failure stress changes, and evaluate sequence association using earthquake-catalog statistics and an epidemic-type aftershock sequence (ETAS) model. The deformation field is elongated NEE–SWW, with maximum displacement toward the satellite of approximately 6 cm. The preferred model has a strike of 242°, dip of 40°, rake of 77°, and maximum slip of 0.21 m concentrated at depths of 4.89–7.46 km, corresponding to Mw 5.91. The joint inversion yields an RMSE of 0.0145 m. Coulomb stress calculations indicate positive ΔCFS at the Aheqi source within the principal rupture-depth interval of 4.89–7.46 km across all three tested receiver-fault geometries. For the preferred geometry, ΔCFS reaches +6.16 bar at the slip-centroid depth of 6.18 km and is close to zero (+0.29 bar) at 10 km, where its sign varies among the tested geometries. At 15 km, ΔCFS is negative for all three geometries. ETAS stochastic declustering assigns the Aheqi event a total triggered-component probability of 82.6% and a background-component probability of 17.4%. Separate attribution analysis yields a 65.7% probability of belonging to the Wushi mainshock’s descendant sequence and a 0.5% probability of direct triggering by the Wushi mainshock. Together with the spatiotemporal compatibility of the two events, these results support a possible interaction within the segmented Maidan–Shayilamusi Fault system, although they do not establish deterministic causation or uniquely identify the physical triggering pathway. The modeled stress pattern also highlights nearby positively loaded fault segments for continued monitoring.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Existing unmanned aerial vehicle fire monitoring methods primarily recognize visible smoke or flame cues but cannot reliably determine whether an observed anomaly is an uncontrolled fire requiring intervention. This study reformulates UAV fire monitoring from a conventional visual-recognition problem into an event-level reasoning and decision task. We propose Fire-Verify-Expert (FVE), a compact vision–language model that jointly performs smoke and fire cue confirmation, real-fire verification, event-type attribution, and ordered response-priority assessment. FVE incorporates fire-management logic into multimodal preference alignment through a multidimensional Group Relative Policy Optimization objective. Its reward mechanism constrains determination correctness, event-type consistency, rationale–decision consistency, and reasoning quality, while imposing asymmetric penalties on operationally costly errors, particularly missed real fires and severe underestimation of response priority. To support this task, we establish a UAV-oriented dataset of 600 images spanning varied environmental contexts and viewing perspectives, including real fires and visually similar controlled or currently non-actionable smoke/fire events. The dataset provides structured reasoning annotations and ordered response-priority labels. FVE achieves an accuracy/F1-score of 0.917/0.915 for real-fire verification, with a recall of 1.000 for real fires, and an accuracy/F1-score of 0.833/0.706 for response-priority assessment. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of FVE in aligning multimodal vision–language reasoning with fire-management requirements, providing a feasible approach for automated fire-event verification and response prioritization in frontline UAV fire-monitoring workflows.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing semantic segmentation often produces spatially concentrated errors around object boundaries, small targets, shadowed areas, and ambiguous land-cover transitions. This study investigates whether learned spatial uncertainty can guide additional supervision of such difficult pixels. We propose Spatially Aware Uncertainty Feedback for Hard Region Learning (SUF-HRL), which refines a maximum-softmax-probability prior through a residual uncertainty branch attached to a SegFormer backbone. Local alignment and boundary concentration objectives encourage a task-adaptive spatial difficulty map; after a warm-up stage, its highest-scoring pixels receive additional cross-entropy supervision. Experiments use the Potsdam and Vaihingen benchmarks of the International Society for Photogrammetry and Remote Sensing and the LoveDA land-cover dataset. Under the controlled SegFormer-B2 protocol, mean intersection over union (mIoU) increases by 0.69, 1.14, and 1.01 percentage points, respectively. Averaging boundary-region mIoU over 3-, 5-, and 7-pixel bands gives gains of approximately 1.44, 1.32, and 1.32 percentage points. Component ablations support contributions from both spatial objectives. Learned uncertainty improves the reported spatial uncertainty criteria on Vaihingen, although it does not achieve the best precision–recall or calibration score. On Potsdam, training time per iteration increases by approximately 68.5% while the reported parameter totals remain nearly unchanged. The additional inference latency is small in the tested configuration. These results support learned spatial uncertainty as a useful training signal for hard-region segmentation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Registration quality in unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) RGB and thermal infrared (TIR) imagery is often assessed using reprojection error, although low residuals at the correspondences used for estimation do not necessarily indicate reliable alignment across the common image footprint. This study investigates Correspondence Coverage Collapse as a diagnostic pattern in which low in-sample error coexists with concentrated correspondences, limited spatial support, and poorer held-out performance. Seventeen RGB–TIR image pairs were analyzed using a two-stage common-footprint normalization and a fixed full-inlier correspondence pool generated by SuperPoint–SuperGlue. Clustered, Random, and Balanced subsets were compared under equal correspondence budgets, with common held-out correspondences used for generalization assessment. At N=80, Clustered selection achieved the lowest selected-point RMSE but showed the highest held-out RMSE, lowest spatial coverage, and largest reference-grid deviation. Confidence-ranked selection showed a similar but weaker pattern at lower correspondence budgets. Continuous analyses further showed that lower coverage and greater distance-to-support were associated with higher held-out error and global transformation deviation. These results indicate that reprojection error alone does not characterize spatial support or held-out performance within the evaluated correspondence pool.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Earthquake-induced landslide susceptibility assessment is important for post-earthquake hazard identification and risk mitigation. However, conventional machine learning models often provide limited insight into their prediction mechanisms. This study applies the Explainable Boosting Machine (EBM) to landslides triggered by the 5 September 2022 Luding earthquake and investigates the nonlinear responses and interactions of the controlling factors. Conditioning factors were screened through correlation and multicollinearity analyses, and the key model parameters were optimized using grid search. The final model included nine main effects and 36 pairwise interactions to characterize nonlinear factor responses and joint effects. The EBM achieved AUC values of 0.987 and 0.976 for the training and testing datasets, respectively, while both Accuracy and F1-score reached 0.920 on the testing dataset. The susceptibility map was classified into five levels using the Natural Breaks method, and the frequency ratio increased from 0.01 in the very low susceptibility class to 6.74 in the very high susceptibility class. Elevation, PGA, and Lithology were the three most important main effects, with importance scores of 1.836, 1.512, and 1.324, respectively. Lithology × PGA and Elevation × Lithology showed the strongest pairwise interactions, indicating that the effects of seismic shaking and elevation on landslide susceptibility were strongly modified by lithological conditions. These results demonstrate that EBM can characterize coseismic landslide susceptibility patterns while directly revealing nonlinear factor responses and pairwise interactions, providing an interpretable framework for understanding the combined influences of seismic, geological, and topographic conditions on landslide susceptibility.

</details>

Abstract (英文): PS-InSAR provides spatially distributed line-of-sight (LOS) deformation measurements, creating an opportunity to forecast dam-surface deformation as a spatial field rather than at isolated monitoring points. However, irregular acquisition intervals, sparse observations, and anisotropic radar pixels complicate multi-step forecasting. We develop SimVP-RD, an observation-integrity- and geometry-aware framework that predicts reference-interval increments, scales them by the actual elapsed time, and integrates them from the latest observed field. The framework combines fixed-support masking, a reconstruction-dynamics objective, native-grid anisotropic convolution, and recursive rollout training. Using 94 real acquisitions of Lianghekou Dam, SimVP-RD achieves an MAE of 3.355 ± 0.024 mm and an RMSE of 4.534 ± 0.034 mm under a matched observed-only 8→8 recursive protocol, outperforming ConvLSTM, PredRNN, and TAU. The corrected Stage-A ablation further shows that the reconstruction-dynamics objective reduces MAE from 3.833 ± 0.111 to 3.549 ± 0.086 mm. On the native PS grid, the 3 × 7 convolution retains all 2170 valid locations and provides a more balanced physical receptive field than the conventional 3 × 3 kernel. Spatial and interval-stratified diagnostics further characterize how forecast errors vary across prediction leads and spatial contexts. These results establish SimVP-RD as a support-preserving approach to multi-step LOS deformation-field forecasting under irregular satellite sampling.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate road extraction from high-resolution satellite imagery is important for transportation planning, infrastructure monitoring, and geospatial updating, but blurred boundaries and fragmented segments still limit practical large-area applications. This study proposes EORENet, an edge-optimized road extraction network for 2 m GF-1 imagery and large-area road product generation. EORENet decouples road-surface segmentation and road-edge delineation into task-specific branches, introduces deformable convolution for adaptive spatial aggregation, and uses a dual-task cross-feature fusion module to exchange semantic and boundary cues. A GF-1 road sample set, the Cangling Road Dataset, was constructed and evaluated together with the Ottawa and DeepGlobe Road datasets. Compared with eight representative methods, EORENet achieved the best overall IoU, F1 score, and edge-buffered F1 score; on the Cangling dataset, it reached 55.11% IoU, 71.06% F1, and 56.21% edge-buffered F1. The trained model was then applied to a spatially independent large-area GF-1 inference collection. Scene-stratified product-level assessment with 1200 independently annotated 512 × 512 patches showed systematic scene-dependent differences, with F1 scores ranging from 62.80% in mountainous forest areas to 74.57% in high-density urban cores and a balanced scene-wise mean F1 of 69.48%. City-scale extraction over four metropolitan regions further visually illustrated the workflow’s potential for large-area road-surface product generation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): UAV-borne LiDAR segmentation of transmission corridors is hindered by the anisotropic geometry of elongated conductors, semantic ambiguity near component junctions, and the loss of sparse insulator evidence during hierarchical sampling. To address these challenges, we propose TSD-LitePT, a three-stage extension of LitePT. First, Learnable Axial Rotary Position Encoding learns independent scales for the x-, y-, and z-axis rotary phases, adapting positional sensitivity to corridor geometry. Second, Transition-Modulated Semantic Refinement derives a segmentation-driven point-wise response from decoded features and uses it to gate semantic residuals, refining ambiguous representations without boundary annotations. Third, Sparse-Target Detail Calibration uses decoded semantics, normalized coordinates, and transition context to calibrate shallow high-resolution features, then aggregates serialized-neighborhood evidence into an insulator prior. A bounded, confidence-aware residual injects this prior into the insulator logit while suppressing changes at confident non-target points. Across three runs on the original five-class corridor dataset, TSD-LitePT achieved an mIoU of 0.9200±0.0003 and an insulator IoU of 0.7236±0.0016 (mean ± standard deviation), compared with 0.8818±0.0061 and 0.5548±0.0435, respectively, for LitePT. After separate retraining on an independent transmission corridor, TSD-LitePT achieved an mIoU of 0.8909, compared with 0.8653 for LitePT. The largest improvement occurred for insulators, while power-line and ground performance remained close to the baseline. These results show that coordinating positional adaptation, semantic refinement, and controlled detail recovery improves minority-component segmentation without disturbing dominant classes.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Seasonal basin management requires knowing when ecosystem services coincide and how their configurations differ among subbasins, information that annual summaries can obscure. We integrated monthly remote sensing, meteorological, soil, and terrain data to estimate carbon fixation (CF), water conservation (WC), soil retention (SR), and wind erosion prevention (WEP) in 18 Yongding River subbasins in 2018, 2020, and 2023. Seasonal association networks and a self-organizing map characterized 648 subbasin–month service profiles and their bundle histories. Of 648 monthly bundle labels, 237 (36.6%) differed from the label of the corresponding annual mean service vector. The high-CF–WC–SR bundle occurred in 26 monthly profiles, including 19 of 54 July observations, whereas subbasin 4 retained a WEP-oriented bundle in 30 of 36 sampled months. In the three-year mean seasonal profiles, 12 of 18 subbasins shared a summer CF-enhanced pathway; the other six followed alternative pathways. CF, WC, and SR were generally higher in summer, while WEP peaked in April and was more prominent in western and southwestern subbasins. Seasonal networks showed the strongest screened association for winter SR–WC, while the summer WC–WEP confidence interval crossed zero. Together, monthly bundle histories and seasonal association networks identify shared summer assessment windows and subbasin-specific priorities for spring wind exposure and service-bundle transition monitoring, providing spatially and temporally explicit evidence for differentiated seasonal management in the Yongding River Basin.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We analyse three decades of ground displacement time series at Somma–Vesuvio volcano (Italy), derived from a multi-mission Differential Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (DInSAR) dataset combining ERS/ENVISAT (1993–2010), RADARSAT-2 (2010–2015), and Sentinel-1 (2015–2024) acquisitions. The DInSAR measurements are referenced to a site at Marigliano, approximately 12 km north of the summit, and are validated against continuous GNSS observations and analysed at representative sites spanning the summit area, the circum-Vesuvian ring, eastern Naples, and the volcanic apron. In addition, we apply Variational Bayesian Independent Component Analysis jointly to quasi-vertical and eastward displacements within 16 km of the summit. The results identify (i) a spatially confined, exponentially decaying subsidence transient along the circum-Vesuvian ring, previously identified from shorter ERS/ENVISAT time series; (ii) deformation in eastern Naples consistent with post-industrial groundwater rebound; (iii) a marked change in the regional deformation regime around 2001–2002, also previously identified from shorter ERS/ENVISAT time series; and (iv) a progressive subsidence slowdown after approximately 2014–2015, particularly along the southern flank. Together, the spatial and temporal coherence of these signals with published seismic, tilt, fumarolic, and groundwater–geochemical observations supports the interpretation of a persistent deep-seated contribution to the long-term deformation of the volcano, while the vbICA decomposition demonstrates that substantial shallow and anthropogenic contributions are superimposed on the volcanic signal. More generally, the results show that long-term multi-mission DInSAR combined with statistical source separation can help disentangle coexisting deformation processes in complex volcanic environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Delineating early cover-crop vegetation in unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) imagery requires preserving slender leaves amid cast shadows, desiccated residue, and heterogeneous soil. Weak foliage contrast and leaf-like background edges make appearance and geometry jointly important. We propose the Shadow, Topology, and Chromatic-aware Prior Feature Topology Fusion Network (STC-PFTF-Net), which integrates structure-guided reconstruction with complementary appearance representations. A coherence-guided tangent–normal decomposition preserves tangential sampling displacement and attenuates displacement across local structures. Chromatic–photometric encoding, directional wavelet filtering, and local connectivity refinement support this reconstruction throughout the feature hierarchy. On the 32 validation tiles of the Residue-rich Cover-Crop UAV Dataset, drawn from an area spatially separated from training, STC-PFTF-Net achieves 77.71% foreground intersection over union (IoU), 50.88% Boundary IoU, 83.84% centerline Dice (clDice), and 87.35% F1. At a common decision threshold, the complete model also exceeds the comparison architectures in all four measures. Spatial analyses associate its higher boundary recall with vegetation–background transitions and identify low-greenness foliage as a distinct source of omission. These findings show the value of combining appearance information with explicit reconstruction geometry for fine-scale canopy delineation. With 2.12 million parameters, the model offers a compact representation for UAV-derived vegetation mapping and subsequent canopy-cover assessment.

Abstract (英文): High-resolution satellite imagery supports scalable mapping, but dense-to-vector pipelines can misrank overlapping candidates and retain local geometric errors. We present SAGE-Map, a framework for structure–appearance guided calibration and geometry refinement built on our SAM-Road-based pipeline for OpenSatMap20. The baseline extracts Lane, Curb, and Virtual polylines with normal-displacement correction. SAGE-Map combines structural scoring with candidate-aligned DINOv2 evidence to rescore Lane candidates, then trims unsupported endpoints while preserving candidate identity, class, and count. On 393 validation tiles, SAGE-Map achieves 29.27, 31.33, 34.06, and 36.06% Chamfer average precision at 0.9, 1.5, 3.0, and 4.5 m, respectively, and 46.78% mean intersection over union (mIoU). Under our reconstructed implementation of the published protocol, these point estimates are higher than reported UniMapGen aggregates; no paired statistical comparison with UniMapGen is available. Paired bootstrap intervals for gains over our baseline are positive through 3.0 m but cross zero at 4.5 m. Post hoc component comparisons associate most of the strict-tolerance gain with rescoring and the mIoU gain with endpoint trimming. Additional geometric analysis shows lower forward distance but worse symmetric distance and reduced GT length coverage after trimming. The gains therefore concern the evaluated ranking and overlap metrics, rather than general geometric completeness.

Abstract (英文): The use of Earth Observation data for monitoring of aquatic and coastal ecosystems has expanded significantly, yet gaps remain in the spectral and thermal capabilities needed to detect and characterize liquid discharges. This study evaluates the potential of simulated pre-launch CHIME and LSTM observations, integrated with operational Sentinel-2 and Landsat 8/9 data, for water-quality anomaly detection across diverse aquatic environments, demonstrating that the simulated datasets are sufficiently realistic to reproduce key water-quality and thermal indicators and support pre-operational application development prior to mission launch. A multi-stage workflow was applied: initial anomaly detection with multispectral imagery, thermal verification using LSTM-simulated brightness temperatures processed via linear and quadratic split-window algorithms, and spectral characterization of turbidity and spectral signatures with atmospherically corrected CHIME-simulated data. Results showed consistent spatial patterns of turbidity and surface temperature between simulated and operational products, with CHIME resolving finer spectral features of suspended matter and the quadratic LSTM model achieving low RMSE relative to Landsat thermal references. These findings demonstrate the cross-sensor consistency and complementary information provided by simulated CHIME and LSTM products, supporting their use in the pre-launch development of future multisensor workflows for detecting and characterizing optical and thermal water anomalies. Identification of specific contaminants and attribution of anomalies to individual discharge sources were not assessed in this study.

Abstract (英文): Artificial islands developed through large-scale coastal reclamation require continuous monitoring to ensure long-term geotechnical stability and infrastructure safety. The results indicate cumulative settlements of up to 20 mm, with spatial variability linked to structural configuration, wave exposure, and loading conditions. Abstract (英文): Leaf radiative transfer models provide a physical basis for leaf trait retrieval, but compensating parameter effects and measurement discrepancies constrain estimation accuracy and uncertainty characterization. This study proposes Training-Informed Differentiable Bayesian Inference (TI-DBI). This method integrates training-derived joint lognormal priors, wavelength-specific spectral biases, and residual scales into a differentiable PROSPECT-D model, enabling joint parameter inference via the No-U-Turn Sampler (NUTS). Evaluated on multi-source datasets, TI-DBI yields relative root mean square errors (rRMSEs) of 27.31%, 33.72%, 16.22%, and 37.66% for chlorophyll (Cab), carotenoids (Car), equivalent water thickness (Cw), and leaf dry mass per area (Cm). Outperforming an uncalibrated Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) baseline for all traits and partial least squares regression (PLSR) for Cab, Car, and Cw, these results confirm the specific value of the data-informed probabilistic framework. Beyond point-estimation, TI-DBI substantially improves uncertainty characterization. It yields narrower interval widths and lower interval scores than the uncalibrated baseline, with prediction interval coverage probability (PICP) of the 95% highest density intervals (HDIs) reaching 93.14%, 96.46%, 89.62%, and 86.22% for Cab, Car, Cw, and Cm, respectively. Ablation and sensitivity studies confirm that both the learned priors and spectral error calibration drive these performance gains across varying training fractions and spectral configurations. TI-DBI strictly preserves the forward model’s physical structure while providing a robust, practical framework for jointly evaluating trait estimation accuracy and posterior uncertainty.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We integrate Sentinel-1 P-SBAS InSAR (2019–2023), continuous GNSS and Sentinel-2 multitemporal urban-footprint data to characterize surface deformation in three Chilean coastal cities (30–38°S) on contrasting substrates: La Serena, Valparaíso and Concepción. A regional velocity field emerges, modulated by postseismic relaxation of the 2010 Maule and 2015 Illapel earthquakes. After GNSS correction, line-of-sight (LOS) velocities reach −30.5 mm/yr in Concepción, and points with |LOS| > 5 mm/yr are about eight times more frequent in Valparaíso (3.1%) and Concepción (3.3%) than in La Serena (0.4%), independently of point density. Negative velocities concentrate on Quaternary deposits at peripheral expansion fronts, including wetland margins in Concepción, with basement and urban cores stable. By 2020, built-up area on mapped wetlands reached 48, 7 and 3 ha in Concepción, Valparaíso and La Serena, respectively, rising to 60, 7 and 4 ha by 2030 (1.6%, 0.3%, 0.2%). The spatial distribution of deformation is therefore spatially associated with unconsolidated, low-lying substrates rather than with tectonic position, although groundwater extraction cannot be excluded as a contributing mechanism. Substrates of the same type failed in Concepción in 2010; continued expansion onto low-lying wetlands may increase the exposure of the built environment to both slow ground deformation and earthquake-induced ground failure, which warrants further geotechnical assessment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ground-based and satellite observations differ in temporal sampling and spatial representativeness, making collocation strategy important for satellite–ground comparisons. We analyzed MAX-DOAS observations in Shihezi, Xinjiang, China, from 5 June to 31 August 2023 together with Sentinel-5P/TROPOMI tropospheric NO2 vertical column density (VCD) products. Five temporal windows (closest observation, ±30 min, ±1 h, ±1.5 h, and ±2 h) and five spatial schemes (nearest pixel, 3 × 3- and 5 × 5-pixel averages, and across-track and along-track adjacent three-pixel averages) formed 25 collocation configurations. NO2 VCD increased during the morning and gradually decreased in the afternoon, while a representative enhancement episode during 12–16 July was concentrated mainly below 1 km. Across the 25 configurations, R ranged from 0.718 to 0.852, whereas NMB ranged from −42.84% to −32.33%, demonstrating that relatively strong temporal correlation did not imply close agreement in absolute VCD. Wider temporal windows generally reduced RMSE and MAE but did not produce corresponding increases in R. The ±30 min window combined with the along-track three-pixel average produced the highest R among the tested configurations and showed comparatively stable performance, although it was not statistically superior to all alternatives. Applying the TROPOMI tropospheric averaging kernel to the MAX-DOAS profiles increased R from 0.852 to 0.879 and reduced the magnitude of NMB from 33.76% to 5.04%. Nearest-pixel-center distance (0.40–6.81 km) was only weakly associated with the relative absolute difference (R = 0.31). These findings demonstrate that collocation choices materially affect TROPOMI–MAX-DOAS consistency and should be evaluated by jointly considering physical representativeness and multiple statistical metrics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Selection of an adequate sampling method is perhaps one of the most crucial stages of any survey. Researchers and decision-makers often face difficulties in selecting appropriate sampling techniques and sample sizes in relation to the specific research problem and the desired accuracy of estimates. Although general rules of thumb are relatively well established, the optimization of these elements is often based on rough approximations. Direct comparisons of the accuracy of different sampling methods in large-scale, real-world forest inventories are, in most cases, not practically feasible. Consequently, some studies rely on resampling limited observational datasets, whereas others generate artificial populations from which predefined samples can be drawn to evaluate sampling efficiency. Nevertheless, such populations tend to be simplified representations of the often complex structures of real forests. This study proposes an approach that could serve as a bridge between real and artificial data. Specifically, the efficiency of three popular sampling designs—simple random sampling (SRS), systematic sampling (SYS), and stratified sampling (StrS)—was compared for growing stock volume (GSV) estimation in a two-phase model-based forest inventory. The comparisons were performed using semi-synthetic forest populations, in which only the target variable was generated using a machine-learning approach, whereas all other parameters were derived from real forest inventory and remote sensing (RS) data. Stratification and sampling were performed using hierarchical data clustering and the concept of structurally guided sampling (SGS), which, in addition to sampling in geographical space, enables sampling in the multidimensional space of auxiliary variables. The proposed stratification technique allows for potentially more effective delineation of strata than predefined spatial layers, which should be crucial for the overall inventory performance. The results showed that hierarchical stratification (clustering) combined with SGS in the airborne laser scanning (ALS) data space was competitive with SRS and SYS, and could even outperform them at lower sampling intensity levels. The convergence zone in the accuracy and precision of SGS-GSV estimates could be reached at lower sampling intensities than with the classical sampling methods. These results demonstrate a potential for optimizing sampling in ALS model-based forest inventories and highlight opportunities for reducing sample sizes, without practically compromising the accuracy and precision of GSV estimates at the forest district level. Nonetheless, further validation on real-world datasets is needed, as synthetic populations may not fully capture the heterogeneity of actual forest structures, which would most likely require more intensive sampling. Therefore, the results presented in this study should be regarded as indicative of potential trends rather than as definitive guidelines for conducting remote sensing-supported forest inventories.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Drygalski Ice Tongue is a tide-influenced floating extension of the David Glacier in East Antarctica, where field observations are constrained by polar logistics. We investigated how horizontal ice motion and vertical tidal response vary along and across the tongue. To extend earlier observations, we analysed a 2025–2026 Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) campaign comprising 12 stations and 248 station-days. Kinematic GNSS coordinate series were produced with Bernese 5.4 and subsequently analysed under four stochastic covariance descriptions for horizontal and apparent vertical-rate estimation. Comparisons with a regional Mario Zucchelli Station (MZS)/Terra Nova Bay tide reference characterized station-level up-component response to tidal forcing, and two tide-removal paths were evaluated for apparent vertical-rate analysis. Floating-tongue speeds increased downstream, spanning 663.9–749.4 m/yr, with a clockwise turn toward the southeast and north-to-south differences in fitted campaign rates. The main horizontal patterns persisted across all four tested covariance descriptions. Vertical tidal response was weak in the near-grounding group, spatially heterogeneous in the transition sector, and near-unity downstream. Across the four covariance descriptions, neither tide-removal approach yielded a model-robust apparent vertical-rate field. The campaign resolved spatial patterns of horizontal motion and vertical tidal response, while covariance testing identified the limits of apparent vertical-rate inference from short GNSS records.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Forest fires are an important disturbance to the carbon cycle of terrestrial ecosystems and a major source of regional carbon emissions. This study used the emission factor method to estimate forest fire CO2, CO, and CH4 emissions in Heilongjiang Province from 2001 to 2022. The geographically and temporally weighted regression (GTWR) model was applied to examine spatiotemporal heterogeneity in the relationships between driving factors and CO2 emissions. Cumulative CO2, CO, and CH4 emissions were 72.95 Tg, 5171.73 Gg, and 246.54 Gg, respectively. Mean emissions per hectare of burned forest were 32.02, 2.27, and 0.108 Mg ha−1 for CO2, CO, and CH4, respectively. Emissions of all three gases showed significant downward trends (p < 0.001) and a bimodal monthly pattern with spring and autumn peaks. Deciduous broadleaf forests contributed 95.6% of CO2 emissions. Spatially, CO2 emissions were higher in the north than in the south and exhibited significant spatial clustering. The GTWR model achieved an adjusted R2 of 0.585, and fractional vegetation cover was the main influencing factor among the variables examined. Forest fire carbon emission estimates remain subject to some uncertainty. The results provide scientific support for regional forest fire risk management and forest fire carbon emission mitigation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Snow alters land–atmosphere energy and moisture exchanges via albedo and hydrological effects, markedly impacting subseasonal-to-seasonal climate variability. While Earth observations and model-based products form the foundation for investigating snow–climate interactions, comprehensive assessments of snowmelt, which directly reflects snow-induced hydrological and thermal anomalies, remain scarce compared to those of conventional snow mass variables. Benchmarked against the satellite–in situ blended GlobSnow data, this study comprehensively evaluates the discrepancies and consistencies of Eurasian spring snowmelt (spring snow water equivalent difference, SSD) across six multi-source datasets (SnowCCI, JRA-3Q, ERA5-Land, MERRA-2, GLDAS-NOAH, and FLDAS) during 1982–2014. The results reveal that satellite-based SnowCCI and JRA-3Q exhibit the highest consistency with GlobSnow in reproducing the spatial distribution, temporal variability, and dominant east–west dipole mode of Eurasian SSD. Specifically, they accurately capture its clear latitudinal zonation and a distinct dipole-like trend, characterized by decreasing SSD over western Eurasia and increasing SSD over eastern Eurasia. Conversely, ERA5-Land and MERRA-2 reasonably capture the temporal evolution of SSD but tend to overestimate variability amplitudes. GLDAS-NOAH exhibits relatively weak regional heterogeneity and underestimates SSD variability, whereas FLDAS tends to overestimate variability amplitudes and shows pronounced regional dependence. Further analysis suggests that uncertainties in snowfall simulations may be a major source of discrepancies in SSD estimation, although their relative contribution remains uncertain. These findings provide crucial insights for selecting appropriate datasets in snow–climate research and underscore the importance of dataset uncertainty quantification in subseasonal-to-seasonal climate prediction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Precise Mars orbiter orbit determination, high-precision lander positioning and planetary interior parameter inversion are core technical requirements for future Mars exploration. The Tianwen-3 Mars sample-return mission requires coordinated orbiter–lander operations, imposing stricter demands on deep-space radio measurements. The Martian tidal Love number k2 describes Mars’ interior structure and tidal response; its accurate estimation constrains the core-mantle configuration and thermal evolution. This study is a simulation-based observability analysis rather than an empirical determination of the Martian tidal parameters. It establishes a joint framework for orbit determination, lander positioning and tidal parameter estimation by integrating ground range-rate and orbiter–lander same-beam VLBI (Very Long Baseline Interferometry) observations. The high-fidelity dynamical models are adopted to build observation models, accounting for the Martian gravity field with degree and order 120, third-body perturbations, solar radiation pressure, relativistic effects and solid tides. Weighted batch least-squares is used to jointly estimate orbiter states, lander coordinates and k2, and the influence of observation arc length on solution accuracy is analyzed. Simulation residuals match preset noise levels. Adding VLBI data reduces the orbiter’s 3D position error by 7.3–33.9%, with an average reduction of approximately 16.8% and full-arc error below 1.6 m. Longer arcs improve lander positioning, reducing 3D error from 88.418 m (7-day) to 33.349 m (45-day). The retrieved k2 0.152866 ± 0.0487 agrees with the reference value of 0.152. However, the current two-station same-beam setup and arc length yield weak empirical constraints on k2, requiring longer observation spans or improved geometries to recover the Martian tidal parameters from real data. Same-beam VLBI provides complementary geometric constraints unavailable from range-rate alone, supporting multi-spacecraft orbit solutions and Martian interior inversion for Tianwen-3.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mineral prospectivity mapping typically relies on static evaluations of individual research units. Although existing spatial modeling methods capture spatial patterns and relationships, they rarely represent spatial relationships as a sequential process of decision making that depends on the current state. This limits their ability to represent spatial changes and cumulative effects. This study proposes a geologically constrained reinforcement learning framework with dynamic spatial search for mineral prospectivity mapping. The method uses a graph neural network to identify geochemical anomalies. It also integrates favorable geological conditions with surface spectral information to construct a geological environment score. Remote sensing responses further refine the spatial representation and differentiation of favorable geological environments. These scores define an integrated prospectivity field and a local geological constraint that guide an agent to move among neighboring units or stop searching. During inference, each research unit serves as an independent starting state, and its prospectivity is evaluated using both the stop-action value and path-averaged prospectivity generated by the learned policy. The chromite case study in Balikun, Xinjiang, indicates that the proposed method identifies high prospectivity areas showing some spatial correspondence with known deposits and exhibits relatively good predictive performance compared with the selected baseline methods. A comparison with a model that excludes remote sensing information provides case-specific evidence of the contribution of these enhanced geological constraints to prospectivity prioritization when the exploration area is fixed. Ablation analysis and other experiments further support the effectiveness and validity of the proposed method.

</details>

The dataset comprised 162 calibration and 41 held-out validation observations. HYB1 achieved the highest calibration R2 (0.416), whereas HYB2 performed best in validation (R2 = 0.500; RMSE = 0.0198 m−1), followed by MR4B (R2 = 0.486; RMSE = 0.0200 m−1). Bootstrap resampling of fixed validation predictions indicated comparatively lower variability in R2 for HYB2 and MR4B, with overlapping confidence intervals. Leave-one-region-out cross-validation highlighted limited geographic transferability, particularly for HYB2 and MR4B in the West Sea. Applying the algorithm to Geostationary Ocean Color Imager-II (GOCI-II) imagery revealed algorithm-dependent retrieval magnitudes and spatial patterns; accuracy assessment requires concurrent field observations. These findings identify HYB2 and MR4B as promising candidates for regional refinement and provide a foundation for advancing GOCI-II-based monitoring of coastal aDOM(440).

Abstract (英文): Urban fringe areas are highly heterogeneous transition zones where rapid land-use change intensifies conflicts among food production, water regulation, and ecological functions. This study integrated remote-sensing products with meteorological, soil, terrain, and socioeconomic data to assess ecosystem services (ES) trade-off optimization potential (TOP) and identify candidate spatial priorities across the urban fringe areas of Henan Province, China, under 2022 baseline conditions. We used a production possibility frontier–Euclidean distance (PPF–ED) framework to quantify TOP as the shortest Euclidean distance to a fitted empirical Pareto frontier in standardized ecosystem-service space. We fitted the frontiers to selected pairwise combinations of estimated grain production (GP), carbon storage (CS), soil retention (SR), and water yield (WY). TOP measures relative geometric departure from these frontiers. Attainable gains in service supply and the benefits of specific management interventions require separate assessment. A ten-predictor Extreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost) model, combined with Shapley additive explanations (SHAP) and multidimensional partial dependence plots (PDPs), identified key predictors and characterized nonlinear individual and joint response patterns. GP–WY showed the strongest negative association and the largest regional distance to its empirical frontier (D = 0.536). Gross domestic product (GDP) and patch density were the leading predictors, accounting for 20.9% and 20.8% of relative mean absolute SHAP importance, respectively, followed by nighttime light (15.6%). Multidimensional PDPs showed higher predicted TOP under combinations of elevated socioeconomic intensity and landscape fragmentation. Integrating PDP-derived high-response conditions, positive local SHAP contributions, and observed high-TOP areas identified candidate priority zones mainly in the northern, northeastern, and central urban fringe areas. The framework connects distance-based trade-off assessment, nonlinear response interpretation, and spatial prioritization to support differentiated ecosystem management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite video enables continuous observation over wide areas, capturing spatiotemporal information for dynamic airborne target monitoring and aviation activity analysis. Such information facilitates aircraft localization and continuous trajectory tracking and can further support finer-grained aircraft analysis. However, publicly available satellite video datasets with in-flight aircraft trajectories remain scarce, and existing data are largely concentrated on airport-centered observations, with limited coverage of wide-area scenes due to the difficulty of data acquisition and the high cost of trajectory annotation. To address this problem, we introduce SAVAir, a satellite video dataset that combines real remote sensing backgrounds with both real and synthetic aircraft instances. SAVAir contains 1281 videos and 99,960 frames, with 1,284,563 horizontal bounding box (HBB) annotations and 159,045 oriented bounding box (OBB) labels. Moreover, the dataset provides rich information, such as visible-instance masks, aircraft configurations, structural attributes, flight phases, and land-cover descriptions. The synthetic data generation pipeline integrates 3D structural modeling, illumination and material reflectance simulation, atmospheric radiative transfer, optical degradation, and motion dynamics, together with sequence-level product-domain calibration and trajectory constraints, to maintain cross-frame appearance and motion consistency. To the best of our knowledge, the proposed pipeline is the first physics-guided synthesis approach specifically designed for aircraft in satellite videos. The synthesized targets achieve an LPIPS score of 0.1607, lower than Copy-Paste (0.1925), SinGAN (0.1943), and Stable Diffusion (0.2112), under consistent experimental settings. Representative object detectors and multi-object trackers are further evaluated under a unified benchmark protocol, while source-disjoint transfer experiments demonstrate the utility of synthetic pretraining under limited real supervision.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Inverse synthetic aperture radar (ISAR) is important for space target surveillance and recognition. However, under large rotation angles and uniformly accelerated target rotation, coupled high-order migration through range cells (MTRC) and two-dimensional space-variant phase errors cannot be accurately compensated by conventional methods based on small-angle or constant-rotation assumptions, causing image defocusing. To address this, a physics-driven subaperture differentiable back-projection (BP) ISAR imaging network is proposed. It embeds a parametric rotation model with angular velocity and acceleration: taking complex-valued HRRP sequences as input, the full aperture is partitioned into overlapping subapertures, and rotation parameters are regressed by a cross-subaperture parameter estimation network exploiting local scattering structures, temporal correlations, and motion evolution features; a differentiable BP imaging layer unifies rotation transformation, slant-range computation, complex-valued interpolation, phase compensation, and coherent accumulation into differentiable tensor operations, allowing image-domain losses to backpropagate to the parameter estimation module for closed-loop joint optimization of parameter estimation and focusing quality; and a phase registration and quality-guided coherent fusion mechanism adaptively suppresses sidelobes, defocusing, and noise from low-quality subapertures. Experiments on point-scatterer and electromagnetic simulation models demonstrate clear advantages, particularly at low SNR, providing a new path for high-resolution ISAR imaging of large-angle maneuvering targets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Surface solar radiation (SSR) over East Asia has been studied mainly over land, although aerosols and clouds may change differently over the adjacent seas. All-sky SSR increased over the ocean at +2.68 W m−2 decade−1 but showed no significant trend over land. The aerosol contribution was positive on both surfaces and significant over 61% of land and 92% of ocean cells, whereas the all-sky SSR trend was significant over only 8% of land cells. Over land, the cloud and background contributions offset most of the aerosol contribution. The ocean-minus-land difference in the SSR trend, +2.26 W m−2 decade−1, comprises contributions of −0.91 from aerosol, +2.75 from cloud, and +0.42 W m−2 decade−1 from the background. Aerosol optical depth (AOD) declined 1.7 times faster over land, whereas the clear-sky flux over the ocean is only 1.2 times as sensitive to AOD, so the aerosol contribution is larger over land (+2.61 versus +1.70 W m−2 decade−1). Within the cloud contribution, the difference arises from the optical thickness part (−1.13 over land versus +1.52 W m−2 decade−1 over the ocean), not from the cloud amount part (−0.47 versus −0.41 W m−2 decade−1).

Abstract (英文): Marine ecological restoration projects are commonly expected to enhance sediment retention and improve coastal stability, but the morphological changes observed on tidal flats during such works may differ markedly between sectors. This study examined short-term changes in the morphology and vegetation of the Nanhui tidal flat, Shanghai, over a period that coincided with the Lingang marine ecological restoration project, from 2021 to September 2024. Multi-source satellite imagery, CoastSat-based waterline extraction, waterline-derived topographic reconstruction, an unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-derived reference surface, and vegetation-index analysis were combined to quantify shoreline movement, tidal-flat area, intertidal slope, cross-sectional elevation change, and vegetation recovery. The reconstructed topography agreed with UAV-derived elevations, with an RMSE of 0.17 m and an R2 of 0.84. Shoreline change rates measured from images acquired at nearly identical water levels ranged from −4.10 to +5.47 m yr−1. The annual mean intertidal gradient was 0.161° in 2021, 0.180° in 2022, 0.177° in 2023 and 0.170° in 2024, with year-to-year differences close to the propagated uncertainty of the gradient; approximately 60% of the monitored sections showed net erosion. At the same time, localized accretion and vegetation recovery occurred in adjacent sectors. These results illustrate the sector-dependent morphodynamic adjustment of engineered tidal flats and show that satellite-derived topography can resolve short-term erosion-accretion patterns in rapidly changing estuarine-coastal settings.

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral LiDAR (HSL) has emerged as a cutting-edge technique for underwater 3D imaging. Nevertheless, wavelength-selective absorption and scattering of light by water induce severe distortion in the spectral information of submerged targets and further result in color casts. In this paper, the underwater target spectral radiometric correction (UWSRC) method is adopted to correct the spectral reflectance of underwater targets by combining pre-measured water spectral transmittance and range data acquired by LiDAR. This approach obviates the complicated in situ measurement of water inherent optical parameters, which reduces the operational complexity for field deployments of underwater hyperspectral LiDAR remote sensing. Its spectral correction capability is experimentally validated under varying detection distances and diverse aquatic environments, including clear tap water and eutrophic turbid water. Furthermore, a dedicated HSL prototype for underwater scanning and a semi-submerged pod are developed. With this prototype, the 3D scanning imaging distance can be extended to 5.3 m in an outdoor swimming pool. Experimental results demonstrate that the UWSRC method can effectively compensate for the spectral reflectance of underwater targets under different water qualities and detection ranges. Meanwhile, true-color 3D point clouds of a color-calibration board are successfully obtained during long-distance scanning. Error analysis of reflectance inversion is carried out to explore the sensitivity to water transmittance bias and ranging uncertainty. The experimental investigations in this paper verify that the UWSRC method achieves favorable performance in underwater target spectral restoration and true-color reconstruction across different water qualities and detection distances.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Geometric misalignment degrades bitemporal remote sensing change detection, while erroneous registration can harm unperturbed inputs. We propose Change-Aware Registration with Gated Output (CARGO), a plug-and-play module that separates transformation estimation from benefit-based output selection without modifying or retraining the detector. A shared change-aware registration module, trained on pooled source datasets, estimates global translation and isotropic scale. The frozen detector processes the original and registered pairs, and a lightweight selector trained for each detector--dataset combination predicts, pair by pair, which output is more beneficial. Under border-free synthetic perturbations, CARGO improves F1 in all 18 combinations of six detectors and three datasets at the most severe level, by 10.8 percentage points on average. On unperturbed inputs, the mean change is +0.1 points (−0.6 to +1.8 across combinations), compared with −3.7 for always registering. On the WHU-CD mixed test, all six detectors have higher F1 point estimates than both fixed strategies. Retrained ablations support learned weighting and change-label supervision, and the registration module is reused on S2Looking. Selection also benefits RoMa, while CARGO incurs lower runtime and memory costs in the measured DINOv3 pipeline.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Invasive plant species pose major ecological and economic challenges, with effective management dependent on accurate and timely detection. This study developed and evaluated a workflow for detecting flowering-stage Chromolaena odorata (Siam Weed) using high-resolution UAV (uncrewed aerial vehicle) imagery and a YOLOv11 object detection model. UAV images captured at Labelle Downs in the Northern Territory were processed into an orthomosaic and tiled for annotation and training. After excluding training tiles, sub-tile-level assessment yielded precision of 0.99, recall of 0.77 and an F1-score of 0.86; validation tiles remained included, so this was not a fully independent test. Detection maps represented candidate infestation patterns. Model transferability was evaluated without retraining at an independent site near Townsville, Queensland, where precision was 0.76, recall was 0.82 and F1-score was 0.79. A supplementary pooled analysis excluding training imagery found lower precision in the highest ranked near-white-pixel group (0.77) than in the remaining classes (0.98), but could not separate brightness from site, sensor, vegetation and image-quality differences. The workflow provides spatially coherent outputs that could guide field verification and management. Further validation across sites, seasons and phenological stages is needed to establish broader applicability.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Urban development involves both the expansion of built-up areas and changes in urban activity intensity. Using nighttime light (NTL) and built-up land (BUL) data from 2002 to 2022, this study examined built-up area expansion, changes in nighttime activity, and transitions between development patterns across two periods in the Sichuan–Chongqing region at a 500 m grid resolution. The results show that BUL increased by a net total of 4494.52 km2, while NTL intensity increased across a substantial share of the study area. The area of newly developed BUL was 18.08% greater in 2012–2022 than in 2002–2012, whereas the average expansion per grid cell decreased by 17.37%. Meanwhile, NTL increases became more widespread in the second period. Among areas characterized by spatial expansion in the first period, 28.84% shifted to a pattern of concurrent spatial expansion and NTL intensification in the second period, and 17.23% shifted to an NTL intensification pattern. These transitions indicate a clear difference in the timing of land development and urban activity growth. Chengdu showed particularly pronounced NTL intensification in the second period, whereas Chongqing exhibited multiple development pathways, with spatial expansion, concurrent growth, and NTL intensification occurring across different areas. These findings help identify how built-up areas and urban activity evolve along different pathways at different stages of rapid urbanization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): LiDAR is an important remote sensing technology for acquiring three-dimensional spatial information, providing data for object identification, localization, and scene understanding in applications such as autonomous driving. As these systems rely on deep learning models, adversarial attacks have become an important tool for evaluating their robustness. Among attack paradigms, transfer-based attacks are particularly practical, crafting adversarial point clouds on accessible surrogate models and transferring them to unknown target models. However, most existing methods either perturb all points under a uniform constraint or concentrate distortion on critical regions, ignoring spatial variations in geometry and sensitivity and often damaging the structures that recognition relies on. Moreover, they target object-level classification and transfer poorly to scene-level detection. Therefore, we propose MIST (Multi-source Imperceptible Saliency-guided Transferable), an adversarial attack framework for LiDAR point cloud perception in remote sensing. MIST fuses source-model gradients, local geometric variation, and predictive uncertainty into a multi-source saliency score that guides perturbation allocation. Guided by this score, Reverse control tightens displacement limits at high-saliency sites, while Intensity scaling modulates update strength to maintain attack effectiveness. Their coordination allocates the budget adaptively by saliency, improving cross-model transferability while preserving geometric imperceptibility. We evaluate MIST through classification on ModelNet40 and ScanObjectNN, and extend to full-scene 3D object detection on KITTI and Waymo under the same transfer-based setting. Results show MIST improves the transferability-fidelity balance in classification and achieves effective cross-model attacks in detection, offering insights for developing more robust perception models in LiDAR-based remote sensing.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Landsat observations provide detailed land-surface information for investigating aerosol optical depth (AOD) estimation over heterogeneous surfaces. We evaluated the predictive relevance of neighborhood background and local variability and examined sensitivity to model design and input processing. A multisource dataset combined Aerosol Robotic Network (AERONET) observations with Landsat 8/9 predictors, meteorological variables, and ancillary surface data. XGBoost, a pure Transformer, and a hybrid Mamba–Transformer were compared under a common station-level split and 0% training masking. On 11,062 test records from 110 held-out stations, the hybrid model achieved a root mean square error (RMSE) of 0.0709. Paired station-bootstrap intervals for inter-model RMSE differences included zero. Retrained ablations increased the hybrid model’s RMSE to 0.1002 after removing nine neighborhood-mean features and to 0.1067 after removing five local standard-deviation features. Both types of neighborhood information therefore contributed predictive information under the evaluated setting. Additional diagnostics examined conditional errors, water-vapor substitution, target transformation, and compositing order. Estimates for sparsely sampled surface types and high-AOD conditions remained uncertain. A China-wide map illustrated model response on a nominal approximately 1 km grid. Independent spatial validation remains necessary to establish regional accuracy and effective AOD resolution.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate estimation of crop evapotranspiration (ETc) plays an important role in sustainable agricultural water management, precision irrigation planning, and food security under a dynamically changing climate. Physics-based models such as the FAO-56 Penman–Monteith equation have a sound theoretical background but are limited in modeling real-time dynamics in vegetation, owing to their use of static crop coefficient (Kc) tables. Meanwhile, ML models trained between the satellite-derived vegetation indices and crop coefficients are prone to physical bound violations and poor spatial generalizability. In this study, the Physics-Informed Machine Learning (PIML) framework is introduced, which consists of connecting the data from Sentinel-2 multispectral reflectance with the data from MODIS gap-filled time-series and the thermal-based reference evapotranspiration by NASA ECOSTRESS within a hard-bounded residual structure, Kc = clip(Kc, prior + clip(fθ(x), −0.15, 0.15), 0.15, 1.20), where the neural network’s correction term resides between FAO-56 agronomic bounds. Although the held-out validation set (N = 200) was intentionally kept small due to the limited number of cloud-free ECOSTRESS overpasses, an operational telemetry system recorded hourly data from four crop fields at the Sustainable Agriculture Facility (RSRF) in California. Because the same four fields and 25 sectors are visited across both training and testing periods, the experimental evaluation represents an intra-site, short-term temporal forward-transfer test rather than a true spatial generalization. The limited sample size (five cloud-free dates) precluded true spatial generalization methods such as leave-one-field-out or field-blocked cross-validation, so we kept all four crops in the training set. Satellite data for the same field are highly spatially correlated; therefore, we used the ICC (intraclass correlation coefficient) to adjust for non-independence, reducing N = 200 to neff =12.0. Three models were benchmarked: an empirical baseline with an RMSE = 0.3801, a constrained PIML network (RMSE = 0.3263), and a global training-mean predictor (RMSE = 0.3257). Model B removed all bound violations in the physical domain (compared to 14% for Model A), reduced RMSE by 14.1% from the baseline, and produced results similar to the global mean; the design-effect-corrected p-value (0.5492) confirms the pilot comparison is statistically underpowered. When validating remote sensing applications with small, clustered samples, these results show that hard physical constraints eliminate physically implausible predictions during temporal forward projection, while highlighting the need to correct spatial autocorrelation and requiring dedicated field-blocked evaluations for future claims of spatial generalization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multi-source remote sensing provides complementary observations of fault position, geometry, and kinematic features, but measurements differ in spatial meaning, evidence source, and processing history. This study combines multi-source fault characterization with a Fault Remote Sensing Dynamic Ontology (FRSDO) to organize and update remote sensing-derived fault features. DEM, Landsat-9, Sentinel-1 SAR, and GF-1/GF-6 data were analyzed along the middle and northern portions of the Yadong–Gulu Fault using established methods and manual interpretation. Feature instances representing fault-location evidence, geometric measurements, LOS deformation patterns, and geomorphological responses were mapped to fault-object or fault-segment levels and linked to supporting records. A directed acyclic graph (DAG) records processing dependencies and identifies downstream nodes affected by source-data or parameter changes. Changes in interpretation criteria or manually interpreted feature boundaries are linked to corresponding instances and attributes for manual review. A thermal-width update experiment identified affected records, revised three segment-level thermal-width attributes, retained predefined nonthermal controls, and preserved provenance. An equivalent relational implementation reproduced all six updated width estimates with a maximum absolute difference of 0 m. FRSDO provides a domain-specific framework for fault-feature representation, evidence linkage, provenance, and controlled dependency-based updating.

Abstract (英文): Aerial object detectors must resolve small, closely spaced objects while retaining enough context to recognize large structures. RS-Adapter uses an image-level class prior to update intermediate features in a frozen vision transformer. The prior combines a learned classifier, fixed class prototypes, and class-conditional descriptor statistics. Cross-attention links the prior to local features, which are fused across scales and gated before being added to the transformer. The adapted features guide a denoiser that generates box prompts for the detection decoder. On VisDrone, RS-Adapter achieved 39.2% average precision (AP) and 28.4% small-object AP, 3.1 percentage points above DEIM-D-FINE-L on the latter metric. AP50 was 78.3% on DIOR, 84.5% on NWPU VHR-10, and 87.7% on RSOD. RS-Adapter trained 16.8 million of its 103.2 million parameters and used less peak training memory than full tuning in the VisDrone comparison.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In microwave coincidence imaging (MCI), radiation patterns with low mutual correlation, generated by an appropriately selected frequency set, provide spatial diversity for high-resolution imaging. For moving targets, however, residual state-prediction errors after motion compensation can still cause a mismatch between the echoes and the observation matrix, degrading imaging performance despite this spatial diversity. To improve imaging robustness under this mismatch, frequency-set selection is guided by a conditional upper bound on the scattering-coefficient recovery error. This bound accounts for both the sensitivity of the observation matrix to state-prediction errors and the distinguishability of sparse scatterers. The state-prediction-error tolerance is chosen such that this bound does not exceed a prescribed recovery-error threshold. The frequency set is then selected by maximizing the minimum tolerance over the prescribed observation conditions. The resulting frequency set is used for closed-loop tracking and imaging with a frequency-diverse metasurface. During closed-loop operation, the predicted state is used to form the observation matrix, and the image centroid is fed back to update the state estimate. This update incorporates the correlation between state-prediction errors and image-derived observation errors. Simulation results show that, at a signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) of 10dB and an x-direction offset of 0.08m, the success rate for resolving two targets separated by 1.83∘ increases from 20% with uniform frequency sampling to 80% with the selected frequency set. For an extended target at 0dB SNR, the peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR), evaluated against a stationary-target reference reconstruction, improves by 1.5dB, and the scatterer-localization root-mean-square error (RMSE) for matched detections decreases by 71.8% compared with uniform frequency sampling. These results demonstrate that the proposed criterion improves resolution, image quality, and localization accuracy in the presence of state-prediction errors, without increasing the number of operating frequencies.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Removing topographic effects from optical satellite imagery is essential for monitoring vegetation in mountainous regions, because uneven solar illumination causes substantial reflectance variation within the same land-cover class. Existing topographic correction tools vary in method coverage, solar-geometry handling, and sensor or data-format requirements. This study presents fortocorrpy, an open-source Python package providing topographic correction methods across optical satellite sensors. The package implements six methods through a common interface: cosine, C-correction, SCS, SCS + C, Statistical–Empirical (SE), and Empirical Rotation (ER). It automatically aligns ancillary rasters to the image grid and calculates illumination conditions for each pixel. In an evaluation against reference images generated using a radiative transfer model, ER yielded the smallest error in both summer and winter, whereas cosine and SCS became unstable under low solar elevation. In Harmonized Landsat and Sentinel-2 (HLS) time series, ER removed most of the difference in near-infrared reflectance of vegetation (NIRv) between opposing forest slopes while leaving flat cropland unchanged. In a comparison of processing time with five existing topographic correction tools, fortocorrpy was the fastest. By also returning intermediate products such as cos i and fitted coefficients, fortocorrpy supports topographic correction across optical satellite datasets and new method development.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Traditional fractional vegetation cover (FVC) calculation methods relying solely on either airborne LiDAR waveforms or point clouds often suffer from degraded performance due to single-modality limitations and spatially uneven point density. To address this issue, this study proposes a multimodal fusion network termed the joint LiDAR waveform and point-cloud network (JWP-Net) and a 2D grid area ratio-based FVC calculation method. First, with a high-resolution digital orthophoto map as an auxiliary reference, an interactive human–machine visual interpretation workflow is adopted to precisely label LiDAR echoes into three categories: vegetation, land, and water. A standardized dataset is thereby established for subsequent data analysis and model training. Second, a novel deep learning network termed JWP-Net, which utilizes LiDAR echo waveforms and point clouds as multimodal inputs, is proposed to classify the laser points into vegetation, land, and water. Third, a 2D grid area ratio-based FVC calculation method is proposed to calculate the FVC based on the classified laser points. To validate the effectiveness and superiority of the proposed framework, experiments are conducted using raw measurement data acquired by the Optech Aquarius LiDAR system. Experimental results demonstrate that JWP-Net achieves an overall classification accuracy of 94.00% ± 0.41% (mean value ± standard deviation of 5-fold overall accuracies evaluated on the untouched held-out test grids), outperforming single-modal models by 1.77–2.52%. Furthermore, integrating DBSCAN-based spatial morphological smoothing on the held-out grids steadily improves the overall classification accuracy to 94.81% ± 0.44%. Moreover, compared with the traditional laser point-count-based FVC calculation method, the proposed 2D grid area ratio-based method reduces the FVC absolute deviation by 3 percentage points on test grids at a 0.2 m grid resolution. The multimodal fusion strategy that combines airborne laser full-waveform and point-cloud data, and the 2D grid area-ratio-based FVC calculation method proposed in this work, offer a novel research approach for high-precision FVC calculation using full-waveform LiDAR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Gas-absent thermal references determine the contrast available for ground-based Fourier transform infrared (FTIR) gas identification, but dense reference sampling increases the measurement burden. We examine compact reference libraries using measured dual-detector interferograms from methane, carbon monoxide, and physically acquired mixtures. Frequency-dependent Planck interpolation and detector-specific scalar approximations are compared with simpler reference substitution using fixed classifiers, measured-reference controls, and acquisition-grouped evaluation. Retaining two endpoint measurements per date/resolution stratum reduces the background library from 48 to 12 acquisitions; across 36 omitted interior backgrounds, Planck interpolation lowers the mean relative waveform error from 0.5512 to 0.1794 for InSb and from 0.1177 to 0.0837 for MCT relative to temperature-linear interpolation. On 54 gas acquisitions, the exact-scene error falls from 52.5% to 23.5% with ExtraTrees and from 53.1% to 29.0% with the neural evaluator; reanalysis using all 65,976 background interferogram rows gives the same trend. Scalar weighting and larger reference libraries produce classifier-dependent outcomes, showing that both the waveform error and gas scene error are needed to select a compact thermal reference library.

Abstract (英文): Sparse lower-tropospheric wind observations over the ocean remain an important source of uncertainty in tropical cyclone (TC) track prediction. This proof-of-concept case study examines lower-tropospheric wind profiles collected by a wave glider equipped with Doppler wind lidar over the northern South China Sea during Typhoon KRATHON (2024). The quality-controlled profiles were assimilated into the Weather Research and Forecasting model using hourly three-dimensional variational rapid update cycling (RUC). Two 72 h forecasts were compared: a control experiment without the wave-glider observations (CTRL) and an assimilation experiment with the wave-glider wind profiles (DA_WGLD). DA_WGLD reduced the 6–72 h mean track error from 75 to 56 km, the root-mean-square error from 83 to 59 km, and the maximum error from 176 to 89 km. The main separation occurred after 48 h, when CTRL developed an increasing northward and eastward displacement, whereas DA_WGLD remained closer to the IBTrACS best track. Initial wind-analysis differences showed coherent spatial structure near the glider and the storm environment. From 42 to 66 h, the northward component of the diagnosed deep-layer steering flow was generally weaker in DA_WGLD, consistent with the reduced northeastward displacement after recurvature. The results establish the feasibility of assimilating wave-glider wind profiles in hourly RUC and quantify the track forecast improvement observed for KRATHON under the selected configuration.

</details>
